## How checks are run
Product checks run in the local-stack snapshot (`/vercel/share/work/app`, refreshed with `local-stack.sh sync`; `app/spike-min` and stale `.next/types` removed first so spike files never mask a product result): `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm test`, `pnpm build` (the repository's required gates, `AGENTS.md`). Database steps run against the disposable Postgres (`127.0.0.1:5433`, identity checked: `invitesion|127.0.0.1|5433|/vercel/share/work/pgdata`). Build = production build without `middleware.ts` (placeholder Clerk keys, P-INV-010), so middleware behaviour is not validated here. Results are quoted from the run, never assumed.

## Entries
(newest last)

### E-001 · B-001 Catalog templates keep their preset (ADR-O01, QA-001)
- **Before (confirmed s09):** `template-sync.ts` stores `{ ...preset, art }` in `templates.default_config`; `create.ts` parsed that with the strict `detailConfigSchema`, which rejects `art` (`unrecognized_keys`), so every catalog invitation started from `{}`. The drafted registry test reproduced the strict rejection for template 0.
- **Acceptance:** for all 64 registry templates the derived config parses and equals the preset; unknown non-template keys still fail; missing defaults → `{}`.
- **Change:** `lib/invitation/template-defaults.ts` (new: `TEMPLATE_OWNED_KEYS`, `catalogDefaultConfig()`, `presetConfigFromTemplateDefaults()`); `lib/invitation/create.ts` uses it and logs a rejected default instead of silently dropping it; `lib/invitation/template-sync.ts` stores `catalogDefaultConfig(tpl)` (same stored JSON as before). Strict schema unchanged.
- **Tests:** registry-wide equality (64/64), negative key, null/array input, source assertion that both call sites use the helpers.
- **Risk / rollback:** L. No data change; existing invitations untouched; new invitations get the full preset. Revert the commit.

### E-002 · B-002 Prices on marketing pages come from `lib/pricing.ts` (ADR-O02)
- **Before (confirmed s09):** homepage and `/pricing` showed a "GRATIS / Rp0" plan card; Standard listed "Semua fitur gratis" and "1 tema premium pilihan" (premium templates are charged the premium price); prices were literals, and the English copy showed "IDR 149,000 / 299,000" while English visitors are charged USD 19 / 39 (`currencyForLocale('en')`); `/pricing` metadata said "Upgrade ke tema premium mulai Rp149.000" (149k is Standard).
- **Acceptance:** no page claims a free plan; every rendered price string equals `formatMoney(priceFor(tier, currencyForLocale(locale)), locale)`; "free" stays only for drafting / preview.
- **Change:** `lib/pricing.ts` `tierPriceLabel()`; `app/marketing-copy.ts` (price literals removed, prices injected by `getMarketingCopy`, draft-and-preview card instead of a free plan, Standard items corrected, CTAs "Mulai buat draf gratis" / "Start a free draft"); `app/page.tsx`, `app/pricing/page.tsx` (no "Rp0"; metadata from the helper); `app/layout.tsx` description from the helper.
- **Tests:** helper equality per locale (USD for `en`), no "Rp0"/free-plan badge/"All free features" in either locale, no price literal in the four source files.
- **Not changed (B-022, P1):** feature claims that do not match shipped features (e.g. "Musik latar & animasi masuk", "Ganti tema kapan saja", countdown on the homepage) — listed in the backlog, untouched here to keep the increment to prices.
- **Risk / rollback:** L (copy only). Revert.

### E-003 · B-003 No unverified religious text in presets; verified corpus scaffold (ADR-O04, QA-015)
- **Before (confirmed s09):** three base presets carried Qur'an translations (QS. Az-Zariyat 49, QS. Ibrahim 40, QS. Al-Hasyr 10) with no source, edition or permission. The new registry gate found five more religious texts in `greetings.prayer`: the basmala in two base presets (aqiqah-khitanan, tahlilan) and three template overrides (`melati-suci` basmala, `halal-bihalal` "Taqabbalallahu minna wa minkum", `kurma-oasis` "Allahumma laka shumtu wa bika amantu" — a shortened form of the iftar prayer, exactly the unverified-wording risk ADR-O04 addresses).
- **Acceptance:** no registry preset carries religious text absent from the verified corpus; existing invitations untouched; corpus with provenance fields, empty.
- **Change:** `lib/invitation/templates.ts` (3 quote blocks and 5 prayers removed, not paraphrased; comment points to the corpus rule); `content/verified-religious-texts/{README.md,corpus.json}` (new, empty, provenance fields and approval steps); `lib/invitation/religious-text.ts` (new: strict corpus schema, `isVerifiedReligiousText`, `looksReligious`, `findUnverifiedReligiousText` — prayers always count, quotes count when text or citation looks religious, so attributed literary quotes such as `midnight-gala`'s stay). The harness reuses the same gate.
- **Tests:** corpus empty + schema refusals (missing source, duplicates, approved without approver, pending entries unusable); gate behaviour (scripture citation, chapter:verse, prayer, literary quote allowed); registry-wide gate; no "QS."/"Bismillah" left in the registry source.
- **Effect:** wedding, aqiqah/khitanan, tahlilan and the three named templates now start without a quote block or prayer line; the greeting section still renders the opening copy. Customer-written content is not checked or changed.
- **Risk / rollback:** L. Revert, or re-enable a text by adding an approved corpus entry (owner-supplied text, source, licence, verifier, approver).

### E-004 · B-004 Event times in the event's own zone (QA-002)
- **Before (confirmed s09):** `components/invitation/public-renderer.tsx` formatted with `toLocaleTimeString('id-ID')` in the server's zone plus a hard-coded " WIB": on a UTC host (the Vercel default) an 08:00+07:00 event printed "01.00 WIB"; WITA/WIT events were labelled WIB.
- **Acceptance:** with `TZ=UTC`, "08.00 WIB", "19.00 WITA", "10.30 WIT"; UTC values fall back to `eventOperations.timezone` (Indonesian zones) or Asia/Jakarta; no day slip; invalid → nothing.
- **Change:** `lib/invitation/event-time.ts` (new; `Intl.DateTimeFormat` with an explicit `timeZone`, label from the same zone, other offsets shown as `UTC±hh:mm`); renderer uses it via `config.eventOperations?.timezone`.
- **Tests:** three zones, UTC fallback (default and WITA), foreign offset, both day-boundary cases, invalid input, source assertion that the renderer no longer hard-codes WIB.
- **Risk / rollback:** L. Revert.

### E-005 · B-005 Editor preview opens the real renderer; theme tab reachable (QA-011)
- **Before (confirmed s09):** "Pratinjau" linked to `/${slug}` (no such route → 404); the theme tab was gated on `config.addons.theme`, a key the strict schema rejects, so it never appeared.
- **Deviation from the backlog wording ("resolves to `/i/<slug>`"), with reason:** `/i/<slug>` refuses DRAFT invitations (the editor's main use) and would count the owner's own visits as guest views. The preview therefore opens a new owner route `/preview/<id>` that renders the same `PublicInvitationRenderer` for any status, behind the owner's session and ownership check, without view tracking and without RSVP / guestbook forms (no guest). `/i/<slug>` remains the public URL. The backlog acceptance line is updated accordingly.
- **Theme tab:** shown for every invitation; the cover image (upload) and headline are editable; template id and palette are shown read-only, because switching them in the editor would let a Standard invitation take a Premium look while checkout prices the DB template (switching within the paid tier is B-036). **New finding QA-026 (s09):** the config PATCH (`app/api/invitations/[id]/detail`) accepts any `theme.templateId` / `paletteId` from the owner without an entitlement check, so a crafted request can still do this; logged as B-041 (P1).
- **Change:** `lib/invitation/public-path.ts` (new, client-safe `guestPath`, `invitationPath`, `ownerPreviewPath`; `public-url.ts` re-exports them); `lib/invitation/public.ts` (`includeDraft` option, used only by the owner route); `app/preview/[id]/page.tsx` (new); `edit/page.tsx` passes `previewHref`; `edit/editor.tsx` (link, theme gate removed, theme tab content).
- **Tests:** path builders; source assertions (no `/${slug}` link, no `addons.theme`, theme always visible, no editable template id, preview checks ownership, passes `includeDraft`, never tracks).
- **Limit:** the owner path itself was not exercised end-to-end (needs a signed-in Clerk session; placeholder keys only); unauthenticated access was checked against the built server (E-007 run).
- **Risk / rollback:** L. Revert; the route is additive.

### E-006 · B-006 CSV export neutralises spreadsheet formulas (QA-012)
- **Before (confirmed s09):** `analytics/route.ts` quoted cells but left leading `= + - @`, so `=HYPERLINK("…")` in a guest name would run when the host opens the file.
- **Acceptance:** `=HYPERLINK("x")` exports as `"'=HYPERLINK(""x"")"`; tab / carriage-return prefixes covered.
- **Change:** `lib/csv.ts` (new `csvCell`, `csvRow`); the route rewritten readably around it (auth, ownership, columns and filename unchanged) and `attending_count` appended as the last column (E-007).
- **Tests:** hostile values, quoting, null/number, source assertion that the route uses `csvRow`.
- **Risk / rollback:** L. Consumers that parse by column position keep working (new column is last). Revert.

### E-007 · B-007 RSVP keeps the host's allowance (QA-005)
- **Before (confirmed s09, live on all three spike arms):** the RSVP action wrote the answer into `guests.guest_count`, so 300/300 allowances went 2 → 1 under the RSVP burst and a decline set it to 0.
- **Acceptance:** confirm 2 of 2 → decline → confirm 2: allowance stays 2, attending 2; migration additive and reversible.
- **Change:** `lib/db/schema.ts` (`attendingCount`, nullable); migration `lib/db/migrations/0009_guest_attending_count.sql` = `ALTER TABLE "guests" ADD COLUMN "attending_count" integer;` (+ `meta/0009_snapshot.json`, `_journal.json`, generated by `drizzle-kit generate --name guest_attending_count`); `lib/invitation/rsvp-rules.ts` (new pure rule `rsvpAttendance`, `rsvpDefaultCount`, status whitelist); `app/i/[slug]/actions.ts` writes `attendingCount` only (event meta keeps `guestCount` = people attending, as before); the renderer preselects the last positive answer, else the allowance; CSV exports both.
- **Tests:** the backlog sequence, clamping, whitelist, source assertion that the action never sets `guestCount`, exact migration text.
- **Database run:** identity checked; `pnpm db:migrate` applied 0009; `attending_count integer NULL`; all 310 existing rows null (no backfill: null = legacy or unanswered).
- **Not changed:** the spike's `next-min` and Rust arms still mirror the old rule (test equipment that reproduces the baseline).
- **Risk / rollback:** L. Revert the code; the column is harmless if left and can be dropped in a later release.

Sprint-2 check run (s09 19:24–19:31Z, after E-008, E-010, E-011): `pnpm typecheck` exit 0; `pnpm test` **85/85** (+17 in `tests/p0-guest-features.test.ts`); mutation check (five deliberate regressions: slug reaching a tokenized guest, no newline escaping in ICS, continuation lines of 75 octets, non-Latin glyphs drawn, opaque links on by default) → every one caught, restored to 0 failures; migration `0010` applied to the identity-checked disposable database (`link_token varchar NULL`, unique index, 310 rows null); `pnpm build` OK 19:30:02Z — `/i/[slug]` and `/i/[slug]/[guestSlug]` are now ƒ, the other 18 Partial-Prerender routes unchanged; new `ƒ /i/[slug]/calendar.ics`, `ƒ /i/[slug]/og.png`.

### E-008 · B-008 Countdown and add-to-calendar (G-025)
- **Before (confirmed s09):** no countdown or calendar export anywhere in the renderer (F-005, F-012).
- **Acceptance:** RFC 5545 file with the correct UTC `DTSTART` from the event zone; countdown hidden after the event; no layout shift; flag for rollback.
- **Change:** `lib/flags.ts` (new: env flags `FLAG_<NAME>=on|off`, defaults `GUEST_CALENDAR` on, `OPAQUE_GUEST_LINKS` off); `lib/invitation/calendar.ts` (new: `buildIcs` with TEXT escaping and 75-octet folding by UTF-8 octets, `calendarEventsFromConfig`, `googleCalendarUrl`, `countdownParts`); `lib/invitation/headline.ts` (renderer's name logic moved out and shared); `components/invitation/countdown.tsx` (client island, `role="timer"`, fixed-size boxes, server-rendered with the request time so hydration matches); `app/i/[slug]/calendar.ics/route.ts` (published invitations only via `loadPublishedInvitationSummary`, no guest data, `Cache-Control: public, max-age=300`); renderer shows the countdown and the two save-the-date links for a future primary event (links only when PUBLISHED).
- **Deviation:** the backlog proposed a new `app/i/[slug]/calendar/route.ts` (never created); that path would shadow a guest whose slug is `calendar`. Side routes end in a dotted segment (`calendar.ics`, `og.png`), which no slug generator can emit (`[a-z0-9-]`; asserted in the tests).
- **No invented data:** a missing or non-increasing end leaves `DTEND` out (RFC 5545 §3.6.1) instead of assuming a duration; the Google link then uses a zero-length range.
- **Live (19:25Z):** guest page renders `role="timer"` "46 hari lagi menuju Akad Nikah" and both links; `calendar.ics` 200 `text/calendar`, `DTSTART:20261220T010000Z` for 08:00 WIB, CRLF only, longest line 64 octets; unknown and draft invitations 404.
- **Risk / rollback:** L. `FLAG_GUEST_CALENDAR=off`.

### E-010 · B-010 Per-invitation link-preview image (G-011, QA-009)
- **Before (confirmed s09):** guest pages had `og:title` only; no `og:image` or `og:description` on the personal link.
- **Acceptance:** 1200×630 `og:image` with names and date for every published invitation; hostile names render as text; `og:description` from the opening copy; no guest data.
- **Change:** `lib/invitation/palettes.ts` (renderer palettes moved out unchanged, plus `paletteHex()` so the image uses the page colours); `lib/invitation/preview-image.ts` (model, FNV-1a content hash for the URL so edits bust caches, `invitationShareMetadata` → description, Open Graph, Twitter card); `app/i/[slug]/og.png/route.tsx` (`next/og` `ImageResponse`, published only, `Cache-Control: public, max-age=600`, overriding next/og's one-year immutable default); both public pages spread the share metadata; `app/layout.tsx` `metadataBase` from `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL` (invalid value ignored rather than breaking every page).
- **Finding during the live check (new, s09):** for glyphs outside its bundled Latin font, `next/og` fetches fonts from `fonts.googleapis.com` with the characters in the query string and emoji from jsDelivr at render time, and draws Arabic without bidi shaping (letters reversed in our test image). Sending parts of customers' names to a third party is a data transfer we have no basis for (UU PDP), so names outside the bundled font are left out of the image (label and date remain) while `og:title` still carries them as text. Bundling licensed fonts through the harness asset gate would lift this.
- **Live (19:25Z):** `og:image` `…/og.png?v=6ix2p5` 1200×630, `og:description` = opening copy, no guest name in any Open Graph/Twitter tag; PNG 1200×630 (27 KB); hostile couple name on the disposable db rendered as literal text, page HTML escaped (0 raw `<script>`); unknown and draft 404. Images viewed: `/tmp/agent-browser/og-*.png` (not committed).
- **Risk / rollback:** L. Revert.

### E-011 · B-011 Opaque guest links behind a flag; legacy links keep working; real 404 (G-005, QA-003)
- **Before (confirmed s09):** guest slug = slugified name and the only credential; `/i/budi-sari/tamu-999` answered **200**. Cause observed in the s09 build: with `experimental.ppr: true` the route is Partial Prerender, so the shell's 200 is flushed before the page calls `notFound()`.
- **Owner decision found while implementing:** `docs/systems/guest-management.md` records "slug polos, TANPA token … (keputusan whyme)". The masterplan did not cite it. Resolution: token minting ships **off** by default (`FLAG_OPAQUE_GUEST_LINKS`), so default behaviour keeps the owner's decision; turning it on is the owner's call. The 404 fix applies regardless.
- **Acceptance:** with the flag on, new guests get `/i/<slug>/<opaque token>`; legacy slug links still render and answer; unknown guests 404.
- **Change:** `lib/invitation/guest-link.ts` (new: 128-bit base64url tokens, `guestLinkSegment`, `pickGuestForSegment` — token first, then the slug of a guest **without** a token, so a tokenized guest is never reachable by the guessable slug); `lib/db/schema.ts` + migration `0010_guest_link_token.sql` (`ADD COLUMN "link_token" varchar(32)` + unique index; generated with `drizzle-kit generate --name guest_link_token`); `findGuestByLinkSegment` in `lib/invitation/public.ts`, used by the page loader **and** `app/i/[slug]/actions.ts` (one rule); both guest-creation actions mint a token when the flag is on; renderer hidden inputs and the WhatsApp share link use `guestLinkSegment`; `next.config.ts` `ppr: 'incremental'` + `experimental_ppr = true` in `app/layout.tsx` (every other route keeps PPR, confirmed by the route table) and `= false` on the two `/i/**` pages.
- **Live (19:30Z):** known guest 200; `tamu-999` **404**; unknown invitation 404; invitation page 200; with a token set on `nama-panjang` (disposable db): token URL 200, its slug URL 404, form carries the token, RSVP posted with the slug → `rsvp=closed` and no write, RSVP with the token → `2|2|CONFIRMED`; token removed → slug URL 200 again. No server errors.
- **Limits:** the flag-on minting path in the dashboard needs a signed-in Clerk session (not exercised; source-asserted); CSV import of guests does not exist in code yet (docs mention it), so nothing to change there.
- **Risk / rollback:** M (rendering mode of two routes). Revert `next.config.ts`/layout/page exports to restore PPR; tokens are additive and only minted with the flag on.

## Session 11 (2026-11-04; baseline `e873680`)
Order and reasoning: LATEST §4 and `sessions/session-11-20261104-0420.md` (05:20Z). Checks: `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm test`, snapshot build (`local-stack.sh sync && build`, without middleware per D-012), live requests against `local-stack.sh start` on :3100, SQL counted with `log_statement=all` on the disposable Postgres (`/vercel/share/work/tools/count-queries.sh`, sandbox-only helper).

### E-012 B-017a atomic view dedupe (DONE s11)
- **Before (confirmed):** `trackInvitationView()` ran select → insert → update as separate statements. The new DB check reproduced the race on the old sequence: 20 concurrent first opens → **19 VIEW rows, `opened_count` 19**.
- **Acceptance (backlog):** 20 concurrent first opens of one guest → exactly one VIEW row and `opened_count` + 1; an open after the window still counts; a tracking failure never blocks the page.
- **Change:** new `lib/invitation/view-tracking.ts` `recordInvitationView()`: one transaction serialised by `pg_advisory_xact_lock(<"VIEW">, guestId | -invitationId)`, then check → insert → increment. `lib/invitation/public.ts` `trackInvitationView()` delegates and stays best-effort (try / catch). Additive index `analytics_events_view_dedupe_idx (invitation_id, guest_id, event_type, created_at)` in `lib/db/schema.ts` + migration `0011_analytics_view_dedupe_index.sql` (generated with `drizzle-kit generate`; the only diff was the index).
- **Tests / checks:** `scripts/verify-view-dedupe.ts` against the disposable Postgres. All six checks pass (one row of 20 concurrent opens, exactly one call reports a write, `opened_count` 1, no write inside the window, +1 after the window, one row of 20 concurrent anonymous opens), and the old sequence produced 19 rows under the same load. Unit test for lock-key separation. Live: the first open of a guest logs one advisory lock + one VIEW insert; a repeat open inside the window logs the lock and no insert.
- **Limits:** the spike consistency re-run (`summarize-spike.py`) was not repeated; the DB check is the direct proof.
- **Risk / rollback:** L. Revert `view-tracking.ts` and the call; the index is additive (`DROP INDEX analytics_events_view_dedupe_idx`).

### E-013 B-014 step 1: one loader call per request, no locale cookie on `/i/*` (DONE s11; step 2 open)
- **Before (measured on the `e873680` build):** a guest open ran `loadPublicInvitation()` twice (metadata + page). Per request: 2 invitation lookups, 2 guest lookups, 2 media and 4 guestbook queries (13 statements with view tracking). The invitation page ran 9 statements.
- **Change:** `loadPublicInvitation` wrapped in React `cache()` with primitive arguments (`includeDraft` became the third positional argument; `app/preview/[id]/page.tsx` and the B-005 source assertion updated). `middleware.ts` no longer sets the `locale` cookie on `/i/*` (`lib/locale-cookie.ts` `mirrorsLocaleCookie()`); the locale still reaches the layout through the `x-app-locale` header.
- **After (same build method):** guest open → 1 invitation, 1 guest, 1 media, 2 guestbook queries (the loader runs once); invitation page → 1 / 0 / 1 / 2. Known guest 200, `tamu-999` 404, second fixture 200.
- **Tests:** source assertions for `cache()` and the middleware guard; `mirrorsLocaleCookie()` unit cases (`/invitations` keeps the cookie).
- **Limits:** the cookie change cannot be observed live here because measurement builds run without middleware (P-INV-010). It is unit-tested and source-asserted only. Step 2 (cacheable invitation-level shell behind a flag, the cross-guest leakage test, spike CPU target ≤ 2 × `next-min`) is not started.
- **Risk / rollback:** L. Revert the wrapper and the guard.

### E-014 Content contract, lenient read path, honest editor (DONE s11)
- **Before (confirmed, `01-codebase-audit/template-editability-s11.md`):** any schema failure in a stored config rendered an empty invitation (`safeParse` → `{}`); the editor offered 10+ inputs the guest page never shows and a guestbook "moderation" toggle that is not enforced.
- **Change:** `lib/invitation/content-contract.ts` (`CONTENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` 1, `RENDER_CAPABILITIES` per section: rendered / storedOnly / hostOnly / caveat; type-only schema import, so the client editor bundles no zod); `lib/invitation/content-read.ts` `normalizeStoredConfig()` (per-section lenient read, dropped key names logged without values); `detail-config.ts` gains optional `schemaVersion` (absent = 1); `public.ts` reads through it. Editor: an amber note under each section title lists stored-only fields, says when a whole section is not shown yet (Music, Instagram Story), and states the moderation caveat; Control Center cards for those sections carry a "Belum tampil" badge.
- **Tests:** `tests/content-contract.test.ts` (11 tests): every schema section classified and nothing else; every `rendered` field is read by the renderer / headline sources; no `storedOnly` field is; notices derived from the map; profile-tab mapping per event type; lenient read (round-trip, legacy `art` + broken `events` dropped while other sections survive, non-objects, future version). Two mutations caught (marking music rendered; rendering `guestbook.moderation`).
- **Live:** a fixture invitation given a legacy `art` key and an invalid `events` entry rendered names and RSVP. Only the events section was missing, and the server logged `config sections skipped on read: art, events`. The fixture was restored afterwards.
- **Limits:** the editor UI needs a signed-in Clerk session, so it was not viewed in a browser in this sandbox. Its logic is unit-tested and it typechecks and builds. The design families that will move fields to `rendered` are not built yet.
- **Risk / rollback:** L. Revert the three modules and the editor note; stored data is untouched (reads only).

### E-015 · B-016 Contrast-safe palette tokens (code s14, verified and extended s15)
- **Before (confirmed s14, from QA-008):** 16 / 18 accents failed AA on their worst surface (sunshine 2.32:1, blossom 2.72:1); muted came from `opacity-60/70` (0.6��0.7 ink weight); dark palettes drew light ink on translucent white cards (1.64:1); the RSVP confirm button was white on emerald-600 (3.65:1).
- **Acceptance (backlog):** every text pair the renderer uses ≥ 4.5:1 (≥ 3:1 large text / glyphs); Lighthouse `color-contrast` passes on a rendered sample of every palette; palette IDs unchanged.
- **Change (s14):** `lib/invitation/palettes.ts` hex tokens per palette (bg, ink, accent, muted, soft, card, field, line) exposed as CSS variables; `lib/invitation/contrast.ts`; renderer, countdown and shared blocks draw colour only through tokens; confirm button emerald-700 (5.36:1). **s15:** the token arithmetic did not cover text laid over a cover photo. Worst case over any image (per-channel blend, black or white image pixel; `worstContrastOverImage()`): names in ink stay ��� 5.54:1 on every palette, but small accent text (cover headline, date) fell to **2.83:1** on 35 of 36 palette × layout pairs (legacy at 25 %, Gapura at 20 % image opacity). Fix: with a cover photo, that text sits on an opaque page-colour chip (`coverText()` in `guest-view.ts`, used by legacy and Gapura); without a photo the class string, and therefore the HTML, is unchanged.
- **Tests:** `tests/palette-contrast.test.ts`: WCAG reference values; every text token on every surface it is drawn on, every palette; opaque surfaces and scheme / luminance agreement; CSS variables and the ivory fallback; fixed status colours; layouts draw colour only through tokens and fade only images; s15: blend and bound against hand-computed values, names over any photo ≥ 4.5:1 for every palette at each layout's real opacity (read from its class), the rendered headline and date carry the chip only when a photo exists.
- **Limits:** token arithmetic plus a proven bound, not yet a rendered Lighthouse `color-contrast` run per palette (the backlog acceptance); hover and focus states are not covered by these checks.
- **Risk / rollback:** L. Revert the palette values and the chip; stored palette IDs are untouched.

### E-020 · B-027 slice 1: RSVP deadline, refusals a guest can see, a name that never clips (s16; PARTIAL)
- **Before (`64701bb`):** `rsvp.deadline` was stored-only. Both actions redirected with `?rsvp=closed` / `?wish=closed`, but no layout rendered a notice for it. Neither action read the host's `enabled` switches, so a crafted POST could write an RSVP or a wish to a section the host had turned off.

Shared `RsvpBlock`: the name is `<dl>` text with `dir="auto"` and `[overflow-wrap:anywhere]`; "Mohon konfirmasi sebelum …" while open; "Batas waktu konfirmasi kehadiran sudah lewat (…)" afterwards (the read-only explanation wins); `role="alert"` notices for refused RSVP and guestbook writes. `app/i/[slug]/actions.ts` reads the config through the page's lenient path and, before any write, refuses an RSVP when RSVP is off or past its deadline and a wish when the guestbook is off. Content contract: `rsvp.deadline` moved to rendered.

Mutation check **13 / 13** caught. Full suite 147 / 147, typecheck 0.

This is an intentional, tested change to the otherwise verbatim legacy layout, confined to the shared form.

### E-022 · Editor uploads refused on save: image fields accept the upload path (s16)
- **Before:** the editor's `ImageUpload` puts the upload route's answer, `mediaUrl(key)` = the relative `/media/<id>/<24 hex>.<ext>`, into the config; `PATCH /api/invitations/[id]/detail` validates with `detailConfigSchema`, whose image fields used `.url()` (absolute URLs only). A probe against the schema refused the path for `logoUrl`, `cover.imageUrl` and `photoUrl` ("Invalid url"), so every save after an image upload failed. Never observed, because no signed-in editor flow can run in a sandbox (P-INV-010). Found while tracing the E-021 logo to its upload.
- **Change:** `lib/invitation/image-url.ts`: `isUploadedMediaPath` (exactly the keys `invitationMediaKey` / `avatarKey` produce, with the extensions `ALLOWED_IMAGE_EXT` stores; no `..`, `//` or query), `isAbsoluteUrl` (what `.url()` accepted) and `safeImageSrc` (an upload path or an http(s) URL, for an `<img>`). `detail-config.ts`: `imageUrlField()` for the five fields `ImageUpload` fills (`couple.bride|groom.photoUrl`, `birthdayPerson.photoUrl`, `ceremonyProfile.photoUrl`, `corporateProfile.logoUrl`, `theme.cover.imageUrl`) accepts an upload path plus everything `.url()` accepted and reports anything else with the same issue (`invalid_string`, "Invalid url"). Widening only: every config that parsed before still parses.
- **Tests:** `tests/upload-image-url.test.ts` (4): every key the storage layer produces (four extensions, invitation and avatar keys, through the real `mediaUrl`) is an upload path; each of the six field paths saves an upload path and still takes absolute URLs; near misses (traversal, query, `svg`, short name, double slash, no leading slash, protocol-relative, other app paths) are refused with the old issue; guest-page sources. Mutation **12 / 12**. Full suite 157 / 157, typecheck 0.
- **Not changed:** the upload route, the storage layer, the editor. No other code parses these values (`new URL` scan; the link-preview image is drawn from text and palette only).
- **Risk / rollback:** L, with one hazard: after a rollback, a config saved with an upload path fails validation again, and the guest page's lenient read drops the whole section that fails (for a cover: palette and family too). Roll back only together with a data fix.

### E-021 / E-022 follow-up: verified on the checkpoint build, without a signed-in session (s17)
- **Persistence path (`scripts/verify-upload-path.ts`, new, 29 / 29):** a fictional logo through the upload route's storage call (`saveImage` keyed to a scratch invitation: type allowlist, size, magic bytes) returns an exact upload path; the pre-E-022 rule (zod `.url()`) refuses that path, reproducing the s16 finding; `detailConfigSchema.partial()` accepts it, `updateDetailConfig` stores it unchanged in the invitation row and in the newest live version, `getDetailConfig` (editor reload) returns it, the guest page's lenient read drops no section; the served guest page requests exactly that path with alt "Logo <organisation>", the 160 × 64 intrinsic size and the category before the headline; the media route answers 200 `image/png` with `nosniff` and the sandbox CSP and the uploaded bytes. Hostile stored values (`javascript:`, `data:`, `ftp:`) still parse for storage (compatibility) and the served page renders no logo for them; traversal, short-name and bare-filename values are refused on save. Scratch rows and the stored file are removed afterwards.
- **Browser (E-021 acceptance, S16-L6):** 50 pages (5 layouts × 5 organiser-mark variants × 360 / 1280), 554 / 554 checks, contact sheets inspected; findings M1–M3 and asset provenance in `family-review-20261104-s16.md` §9.

Either keep `imageUrlField()` while reverting anything else, or first migrate every stored upload path to an absolute URL on the serving origin (`<APP_URL>/media/<key>` in the five `ImageUpload` fields of `invitations.config` and `invitation_versions.config`), check that the lenient read drops nothing on a copy of the data, then deploy. The forward contract stays: storage accepts exact upload paths plus every absolute URL it took before; renderers request only upload paths and http(s) URLs (`safeImageSrc`).

### E-023 · The cover photo goes through `safeImageSrc` (s17)
- **Before (`f2fd128`):** every layout rendered `theme.cover.imageUrl` as an `<img src>` from whatever the schema had parsed (any absolute URL, e.g. `javascript:`, `data:`, `blob:`, `file:`, `ftp:`), and the photo chip (`coverText`) keyed on the stored value, while the logo already used `safeImageSrc` (E-021 limit).
- **Change:** `guest-view.ts` derives `coverImageSrc = safeImageSrc(theme.cover.imageUrl)` once; the five layouts draw the cover from it and the chip appears only when a photo is drawn. Stored values and the schema are untouched (stored-value compatibility); an upload path or an http(s) URL renders exactly as before (`safeImageSrc` returns the URL's normalised `href`, identical for every value the fixture and tests use). `couple` / `birthdayPerson` / `ceremonyProfile` `photoUrl` stay stored-only (no renderer), and gallery images come from the media table, not from typed URLs.
- **Tests:** `tests/cover-image-src.test.ts` (4): uploaded and http(s) covers drawn in every layout; each other scheme renders byte-for-byte the page without a cover; the chip follows the drawn photo; stored covers keep parsing and the lenient read keeps the theme. `tests/design-families.test.ts` composition pattern now requires `view.coverImageSrc`. Mutation 4 / 4 of the E-023 mutants caught (10 / 10 with E-024).
- **Risk / rollback:** L. Revert the view field and the five layout reads; no stored data involved.

### E-024 · B-027: a refusal for a switched-off section is announced (s17; closes the E-020 limit)
- **Before:** a form rendered while RSVP or the guestbook was on, submitted after the host switched it off, was refused correctly (E-020) with `?rsvp=closed` / `?wish=closed`, but every layout hid the whole section, so the refusal had nowhere to render.
- **Change:** `guest-view.ts` reads the switches once (`rsvpShown`, `guestbookShown`) and every layout uses them instead of its own `enabled !== false`; shared `SwitchedOffNotices` renders the same `role="alert"` texts as the blocks right after the cover (where the read-only notice goes) when a hidden section's write was refused. Only `closed` reaches a hidden section; a crafted `saved` / `invalid` shows nothing there. The texts are shared constants, so the section and the fallback cannot drift.
- **Tests:** `tests/b027-switched-off.test.ts` (3): RSVP and wish refusals announced once after the cover and before the programme in every layout, the hidden section stays hidden, a shown section keeps its own notice without a duplicate, no alert without a refusal. Mutation 6 / 6 of the E-024 mutants caught. Full suite **164 / 164**, typecheck 0.
- **Risk / rollback:** L. Revert the component, the two view fields and the layout reads.

### E-025 · B-027 slice 2a: "maybe" as an RSVP answer (s17; B-027 still PARTIAL)
- **Acceptance (the B-027 row, for "maybe"):** "maybe" counted separately; answers stored and exported (CSV neutralised, B-006); per-feature flag; config untouched; answers retained.
- **Change:** flag `RSVP_MAYBE` (default on, like the other additive guest-page features; `FLAG_RSVP_MAYBE=off` is the rollback). `rsvp-rules.ts`: `MAYBE` joins the statuses; `isRsvpStatus` accepts it only when the caller passes the flag (default refuses, as before); `rsvpAttendance` stores `attending_count` = null for "maybe", so a tentative answer never enters a headcount (the count field applies to "hadir"); `rsvpCounts` and the guest-facing `RSVP_ANSWER_TEXT`. The action checks the flag before any write, so switching it off also refuses stale forms. Shared `RsvpBlock`: a third "Mungkin hadir" answer while the flag is on (three columns from 640 px); the last-answer line reads every known status and no longer calls an unknown stored value a decline. Dashboard: a separate "Mungkin" metric and a "Mungkin" control on the guest list; the dashboard action accepts `MAYBE`. Export: rows moved to `lib/invitation/guest-export.ts` (`guestExportCsv`, still through `csvRow`); "maybe" exports under its own status with an empty `attending_count`. `rsvp_status` is a varchar, so no migration; `i18n` labels, schema comment and `docs/systems/guest-management.md` / `docs/DATABASE_SCHEMA.md` updated.

A code revert would leave MAYBE rows that older readers show as a raw status (the dashboard falls back to the value) and that the old guest form would describe as a decline.

## Session 18 (2026-11-05; baseline `052e4b1`, the owner's import of the s17 work)
E-028 and E-029 were written in s17 and reached the owner's checkpoint without a browser or integrated check; s18 accepted them on a production snapshot of the unmodified checkpoint tree (BUILD_ID `h9LUfEUdSaLEkwWA47cOc`, Postgres 16.15, the s18 fixture of 186 invitations), so that evidence describes the committed code. The defects it found are fixed by E-030; E-031 adds the host's question authoring. Session record: `sessions/session-18-20261105-0552.md`.

Window 300 × 225 below 640 px and 364 × 364 from 640 px, full strength, in flow, clipped by the arch, decorative, no text on it. **S17-G1 met:** at 360 × 800 the guest name ends at 739 px on both photo pages (a square window would put it at 814 px) and at 795 px with the long name. Measured limits, not hidden: `corp-wide` 835 px, every photo page at 360 × 640, and 1280 × 800 photo pages at 908 px (**S18-G2**, open; `family-review-20261104-s16.md` §11).

The failure is product defect **S18-Q1** (E-030).

The app on that database, **10 / 10**: every pre-0012 guest opens the page with the questions and no stored answers and still reads back their earlier answer; first answers on a pre-0012 row are stored; no other row changes.

### E-030 · B-027: the defects the E-029 acceptance found (S18-Q1…Q4; s18)
- **S18-Q1:** a long-text answer at the browser's own `maxlength` (2,000 characters with line breaks) was refused, because forms submit line breaks as CRLF and the server counted 2,010. `parseOne` reads CR and CRLF as LF before trimming and the length check, so the limit is the one the guest saw and one line-break form is stored. Answers stored with CRLF before the fix stay readable as they are.
- **S18-Q2:** every question reads label → condition → refusal → control (fieldsets: legend → condition → refusal → options), so a guest learns when to answer before answering; `aria-describedby` unchanged.
- **S18-Q3:** `canEverApply` / `askableQuestions`: a question whose condition chain has a cycle, a missing source, or a choice source none of whose options meets the condition can never count (D-034), so the guest page no longer shows it. The action's rules are unchanged: such answers were already dropped and never required.
- **S18-Q4, read path (final, as revised under D-035 in the same session):** when the strict schema refuses a registration form, `normalizeStoredConfig` reads it question by question. A question that does not parse, or repeats an id (the first stays), is left out, because no reader could ever show it, and `dropped` names `registrationForm.customQuestions`, never values. **A condition naming a missing question stays:** D-034 already counts that question as never applying, the guest view hides it (S18-Q3), and its stored answers belong in the export. A form failing for any other reason is still dropped whole and named as the section. *Superseded first repair (written 06:15–06:29Z, recorded here at 06:35Z, never committed):* it also dropped every question depending, directly or through the chain, on a missing source. The E-030 export re-run (11 / 12) showed the cost: deleting `menu` took the dependent `alergi` column, and with it that question's stored answers, out of the export. D-035 was revised before the commit.
- **S18-Q4, write path and publishing (final):** in `updateDetailConfig`, a save that leaves the registration form alone carries the stored form as every reader sees it (`carriedRegistrationForm()`), so a stale condition cannot block saving another section and is never silently rewritten into a different form. A save of the form itself stays strict: the editor shows the condition and asks the host to choose again (E-031). Any other invalid section still fails the strict parse of the merged whole. `publishBlockers` still refuses a config that some reader would cut (anything in `dropped`, or a stored value that is not an object), but no longer a dangling condition alone. **Owner pages:** the manage, edit and planner pages and `getDetailConfig` read leniently instead of using a strict parse that opened an empty editor; the guest page, the action and the export route already did.
- **Tests:** `tests/b027-questions-repair.test.ts` (8): CRLF, CR and LF at the limit and one character over; the order in all five layouts with a refusal; never-applicable questions hidden, a free-text source kept, hidden answers neither required nor stored; the read repair on the page and in the export, with a deleted source's dependants kept for the export and hidden from guests; other failures dropped whole; the write path (another section saves past a stored inconsistent form, carried as readers see it; the form itself and other invalid sections still refused); publishing past a dangling condition; wiring. **Mutation 15 / 15** of the E-030 and D-035 mutants on the final code (S18-Q1 1, Q2 2, Q3 3, Q4 5, D-035 4; 13 / 13 on the superseded repair). The scratch runner works in a copy of the tree and restores sources by hash.
- **Acceptance on the final build (s18):** snapshot BUILD_ID `hGPHuG02UUAxLS4Q_uyJL`, every tracked product file equal to the working tree except the D-012 middleware; :3100 default flags, :3101 `FLAG_RSVP_QUESTIONS=off`; fictional scratch guests only.
  - **Actions 73 / 73:** the E-029 scenarios, with only the S18-Q1 / Q2 / Q3 expectations updated. A 2,000-character answer with 10 line breaks is saved and stored with LF only; ten questions are shown, with the cycle hidden.
  - **Layouts and keyboard 93 / 93.**
  - **Export and read path 19 / 19** (`e030-export.ts`): the E-029 export checks, plus two D-035 cases. A dangling condition stays in the read config, its column is exported, the guest view hides it and publishing is not blocked. A deleted source written to the row keeps both dependants' columns and stored answers in the export, and neither dependant is asked; every config was restored by digest.
  - **Guest page with a deleted source 11 / 11** (`e030-dangling.cjs`): the seven remaining askable questions in order, with no label or hint of the hidden ones; a confirmed RSVP saves although the hidden `bus-titik` is marked required; the action drops forged answers to the hidden questions; reload and flag off also checked. The first run passed every product check, but its restore bound the config text straight to `::jsonb` (the s17 hazard in LATEST §6) and stored a JSON string. That was repaired in SQL from the string, all 186 configs were digest-equal to the pre-run snapshot, and the fixed script was re-run.
  - **Intermediate E-030 build** (`Vr8MoEKRh3UBgycA-h-a5`, before the D-035 revision): actions 73 / 73, layouts 93 / 93, export 11 / 12 (the superseded repair). Contrast on the final build: `contrast-sweep-20261104-s16.md` §8.
- **Risk / rollback:** L. Rendering and the read path, plus a write path that carries a stored form unchanged past saves of other sections. Reverting restores the strict reads, and with them the empty editor for such configs.

### E-031 · B-027: the host's question editor (s18)
- **Before (`052e4b1`):** the manage panel's registration module offered a wording field, a delete button and "Tambah pertanyaan" (always a text question): no answer type, choices, "required", condition or order. Deleting a question another question's condition named made the save fail with a generic "Gagal menyimpan" (strict schema), and the panel showed "Izinkan pendamping" checked while the schema stores false. Schema and guest page supported every type, choices, "required" and conditions, so only seeded or hand-written configs could use them.
- **Change:** `lib/invitation/question-authoring.ts` (pure rules) and `manage/question-editor.tsx`: wording, answer type (six offered; a stored `multiselect` keeps its entry), "required for guests who attend", choices (add, rename, remove), a condition on an earlier choice question with the answers that make it count, move up and down, delete. A condition can name only an earlier choice question with a written option, so cycles cannot be authored and the form reads top-down; a move that would put a question above its source is disabled. Deleting or retyping a source, or removing its last chosen option, removes the dependants' conditions and says so in a polite live region; renaming an option renames it inside the conditions. Before the PATCH the panel checks the list with `validateQuestions` (per-field messages tied by `aria-describedby`; the schema's limits, including 20 condition values) and with the detail API's own schema (`storedRegistrationForm`), then sends it trimmed with conditions as `in`. The panel lists the registration fields that are stored but neither shown nor enforced (content contract) and shows the schema's plus-one default. A stored condition naming a deleted question (older code, a hand edit; D-035) reaches the editor, is reported on its field ("Syarat harus merujuk pertanyaan pilihan yang berada di atasnya.") and blocks saving the form until the host chooses a source again or removes the condition.
- **Tests:** `tests/b027-question-authoring.test.ts` (10): ids and the limit; sources earlier and choice-only; moves; deletion, retyping and option removal with their notes; renaming; per-field issues and the stored form; **a seeded property test (40 sequences × 80 random edits): every intermediate list stays ordered, acyclic and free of stray options, and every list without issues is accepted by the detail API and asked in full by the guest page**; the server-rendered editor (labels, issue association, disabled moves, offered sources, live region); a stored dangling condition reaching the host and blocking the save; panel wiring. **Mutation 10 / 10** of the E-031 mutants, re-run on the final code together with E-030 and D-035: **25 / 25**. With E-030: suite **213 / 213**, typecheck 0.
- **Not verified:** the signed-in editor in a browser (P-INV-010: the dashboard needs a real Clerk session); the editor is checked by server rendering and its rules by unit tests.
- **Risk / rollback:** L. Owner UI only; the stored shape is the schema's. Revert the two files and the panel.

### E-032 · B-030 Lontar: a saved cover photo without date, time or venue (S17-L1; s18)
- **Before:** Lontar drew the cover photo only inside the date band's grid. An invitation whose primary event had no date, time and venue therefore showed no cover photo at all, although the host had saved one (S17-L1, found in s17 by reading the code). The other four layouts already drew it.
- **Change:** `components/invitation/families/lontar.tsx`: the band and the photo are independent. With both, the arrangement is unchanged: the photo sits beside the band (`sm:grid-cols-[1fr_13rem]`) at the band's height. A photo alone keeps its 4 : 3 frame at every width, full content width on phones and 24 rem from 640 px (`sm:w-96`), with no empty band column, no borrowed height and no binding cord. A band alone is unchanged.
- **Finding S18-E1 (while checking it):** the first fix bounded the lone photo with `sm:max-w-sm`, and the rendered check measured 632 px instead of at most 384. `app/globals.css` sets an unlayered `img { max-width: 100%; }`, and unlayered CSS beats every Tailwind v4 layer, so no `max-w-*` utility takes effect on an `<img>`. The only other image with one is the organiser logo, whose `max-w-full` equals the global value, so nothing else changes. E-032 bounds the photo by width instead. The global rule is left as it is (a shared stylesheet, with the scaffold-copy hazard of LATEST §6); moving it into `@layer base` is a separate change that needs every image re-checked.
- **Tests:** `tests/cover-image-src.test.ts`, new test: every layout draws the saved photo with no events and with a title-only event; Lontar's lone photo keeps its 4 : 3 frame without the band's column, height or cord, and is bounded by a width utility, never a `max-w-*`; with a band the arrangement is as before. ## Session 19 (2026-11-05; baseline `4368f0f`, the owner's import of the s18 work)

### E-034 · B-027 slice 2c, step 1: durable event ids (D-036; s19)
- **Before:** an event had no identity besides its array position; the editor keyed rows by index, could not reorder, and added events without any id; calendar UIDs were `<slug>.<index>`, so after a reorder a re-imported calendar file updated the wrong entry. Per-event answers could not be stored safely (D-034).
- **Change:** `eventItemSchema.id` (optional on the schema, unique within `events`). `lib/invitation/event-ids.ts` holds the one rule: `keyStoredEvents` keeps the first holder of a usable id and gives every other event `ev-<position>` (or `ev-<position>-<k>` when taken), deterministically, so the guest page and the RSVP action derive the same ids from the same stored config; `keyEventsForWrite` gives an event a writer sends without an id a fresh random id (`ev-` + letter + 9 base-36, never equal to a legacy id, so a new event never inherits a deleted event's answers); `rekeyRestoredEvents` is the contract for a future version restore (kept ids reattach; a pre-0013 event takes the live event with the same title and start, else a fresh id, never a position). Read path: `normalizeStoredConfig` keys events before parsing. Write path: `prepareConfigWrite` (moved out of `updateDetailConfig`, unchanged D-035 carry rule) keys carried events by the read rule, keys patch events for write and stamps `schemaVersion` **2** (`CONTENT_SCHEMA_VERSION`). Migration **0013** backfills ids with the same rule for every config that needs no tie-break and stamps version 2; tie-break configs (repeated or malformed ids, a minted id already taken, a non-object element) keep reading through the rule and are written on their next save; saved versions are history and are not rewritten. Editor: new events get `newEventId()`, rows are keyed by id, up / down buttons move events (ids travel with them). Calendar: `calendarUid` follows the id; a legacy `ev-<n>` keeps `<slug>.<n-1>`, the UID saved entries already have.
- **Tests:** `tests/b027-event-ids.test.ts` (11): deterministic read ids, kept / repeated / malformed / colliding ids, reorder, the write path (carried ids persisted, new ids fresh, delete-then-add never reuses), random-id form, schema uniqueness, the restore contract, calendar UID continuity, editor wiring, the migration's rule. `tests/content-contract.test.ts` updated for version 2. Suite **228 / 228** after E-034.
- **Database proof (`scripts/verify-event-ids.ts`, populated database):** migrated to 0012 from a copy of the migrations without 0013, seeded templates, demo and the review fixture (`--palettes --mark-variants --ceremony --questions`), plus 9 crafted edge cases and a pre-0013 saved version; then `pnpm db:migrate` applied 0013. **16 / 16:** 197 invitations compared, **0** whose read-path event ids or content changed, **190** rewritten with ids and version 2 and nothing else (188 fixture rows + legacy + partial), the 7 tie-break / no-op crafted rows byte-identical, versions and guest rows digest-equal, a second run of the SQL changes 0 rows, 14 migrations recorded. Crafted rows removed afterwards.
- **Not verified:** the editor's reorder buttons in a browser (P-INV-010, signed-in editor). Checked by the wiring test and typecheck.
- **Risk / rollback:** M. Data migration on every config with events. Rollback: the code tolerates configs with or without ids (ids are optional on the schema and readers mint the same ones), so reverting the code leaves the ids as harmless extra keys; 0013 itself need not be reverted.

### E-035 · B-027 slice 2c: an answer per event (D-036; s19)
- **Change:** host switch `rsvp.perEvent` (editor: "Konfirmasi per acara") behind flag `RSVP_PER_EVENT` (default on, additive). With the switch on and two or more events, the RSVP form asks for each event a required choice (hadir / tidak hadir / mungkin while its flag is on) and the people coming to that event, then one "Kirim konfirmasi" button. `lib/invitation/rsvp-events.ts` is the one rule set: `parseEventAnswers` (clamped like the single answer; every listed event must be answered, else `?rsvp=events&rsvpe=<id>` names it; fields for unlisted or other invitations' ids are ignored), `overallFromEvents` (hadir if any, else mungkin if any, else tidak hadir; people overall = the largest per-event hadir count), `mergeEventAnswers` (answers for events no longer listed stay stored), `eventRsvpSummary`, `exportedEvents`, `eventAnswerCells`. Storage: nullable `guests.rsvp_event_answers` jsonb (migration **0014**), `{ [eventId]: { status, attending } }`, written by the **same single UPDATE** as status, headcount and question answers, scoped to the guest row the link resolves. A single-answer submission while the flag is on sets it to null (the latest answer wins); flag off neither reads nor writes it; switching the host's toggle off erases nothing. Export: two columns per exported event, read by id. The custom questions' "required for hadir" now follows the derived overall status; with per-event answers the browser requires no question natively.
- **Tests:** `tests/b027-per-event.test.ts` (10): activation rule, clamping and the derived overall answer, stale forms (added event refused by name, single-answer form refused, deleted / foreign ids ignored), reorder + rename keep answers and export columns, kept answers for deleted events and per-entry malformed reads, the per-event summary (no answer = belum menjawab, never a decline), export columns on / off / deleted plus formula neutralisation and RTL titles, every layout's rendered form (three required choices per event, stored answer preselected, one count per event, no single headcount, one submit, the refusal notice), the single form unchanged without per-event answers, the action's single UPDATE / scoping / no answers in analytics. Five existing wiring tests updated for the restructured action (same intent: the flag is read before the write; one UPDATE). Suite **238 / 238**.
- **Browser acceptance (s19; D-012 production snapshot BUILD_ID `-xnj6DKS7v0rSAjC1VXH7`; scratch `tools/template-harness/.out/s19/e035-actions.mjs`, git-ignored):** **18 / 18.** With `rsvp.perEvent` switched on for the five `review-<layout>-full` invitations: every layout at 360 × 800 and 1280 × 800 shows two event fieldsets with required choices, a count each, one "Kirim konfirmasi", no single headcount and no horizontal overflow (10 / 10); real submissions on legacy and Gapura store CONFIRMED, 2 people and `{ ev-1: DECLINED 0, ev-2: CONFIRMED 2 }`; a page opened before the host added a third event is refused with `rsvp=events&rsvpe=<new id>` naming it and writes nothing, and the reloaded form asks all three; after the host swaps the events each stored answer is preselected under its own event; other invitations' guests unchanged; configs restored byte-identical and guest RSVP fields restored (served pages still add VIEW rows and `opened_count`, as in every browser check).
- **Not verified yet:** the dashboard per-event table and the editor switch in a browser (P-INV-010); the flag-off server (covered by the rendered tests only). Per-person attendance (which member of a household attends which event) and per-event eligibility (`secondaryEvents.eligibleGuestIds`) are out of scope.
- **Risk / rollback:** M. Rollback: `FLAG_RSVP_PER_EVENT=off` restores the single answer exactly; the column stays and is ignored.

### E-037 · S18-E1: the global image rule no longer defeats `max-w-*` (s19)
- **Before:** `app/globals.css` ended its globals with an unlayered `img { max-width: 100%; }`. Unlayered CSS outranks every `@layer` rule regardless of specificity, so no `max-w-*` utility took effect on an `<img>` (E-032 had to bound Lontar's photo by width instead).
- **Change:** the rule is removed, with a comment saying why; Tailwind's preflight already caps images at 100% inside `@layer base`. Edited with a checked script and confirmed with `$GIT_RESEARCH diff` (scaffold-copy hazard). E-032's width bound stays.
- **Evidence:** on the old build a layered `max-width: 12px` utility on an `<img>` computed to `100%` (bug reproduced); on the rebuilt snapshot (BUILD_ID `QQQvkJn8YNtOqji6XZ795`, scratch `tools/template-harness/.out/s19/e037-images.mjs`) **180 / 180** checks over the photo, photo-low, corporate, corp-wide, corp-rtl and full variants of all five layouts at 360 × 800 and 1280 × 800: the probe computes to `12px`, no horizontal overflow, every one of the 30 measured image boxes inside its container and the viewport. `tests/s18-e1-image-cascade.test.ts` guards against a top-level `img` rule.
- **Limit:** the review cover files were not regenerated in s19 (the fixture's cover URLs answered 404 on this snapshot), so photo framing with loaded pixels was not re-inspected; the rule's removal changes no current rendering by construction (the only `max-w-*` on an image is the logo's `max-w-full`, equal to preflight), which the box measurements agree with.
- **Risk / rollback:** L. One CSS line; restore it to roll back.

It is not access control on the file: `/media/<invitation>/<24 hex>.<ext>` is served to anyone holding the URL, with `cache-control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable` (`app/media/[...path]/route.ts`). ## Session 21 (2026-11-06; baseline `f4473e7`, the owner's import of the s20 work)

Fresh sandbox; checkpoint reproduced (typecheck 0, **263 / 263**). Two decorative separator comments in `lib/db/schema.ts` carried U+FFFD from the source snapshot (lines 180 and 337); both restored to the intact 45-character rule by a checked script, no definition touched (`4368f0f` had them intact).

### B-014 step 2 evaluation: a cached invitation shell, measured and not shipped (s21, D-039)
- **Mechanism checked first:** Next.js `15.6.0-canary.59` supports `'use cache'` behind `experimental.useCache` (default handler: in-memory LRU, 50 MB, an entry missing after its `revalidate`; key = the encoded `[buildId, function id, arguments]`, not hashed). Enabling it **fails the build** on three routes that declare `export const runtime = 'nodejs'` (upload, payment webhook, `/media`); the experiment removed those lines in a scratch copy only.
- **Reuse proven:** in the force-dynamic, PPR-opted-out guest route, `NEXT_PRIVATE_DEBUG_CACHE` showed 50 lookups over five pages: 5 misses (first request each) and **45 / 45 hits**. A key built from full rows misses whenever a volatile column changes (the guest's `opened_count` after a view), so any real key must name render inputs only.
- **Upper bound measured:** the whole guest page in one `'use cache'` scope keyed by every input (more than any shell with per-guest holes can cache), against the unchanged build, same host, interleaved, 3 × 300 requests at concurrency 4 over five layouts: server CPU **29.6 → 28.6 ms per view** (rounds 28.0–30.2 vs 27.1–28.9, overlapping). On a hit Next still replays the cached Flight stream into the response and SSR still parses and renders it.
- **Where the cost is (CPU profile, 400 views):** SSR's Flight client parsing the RSC payload (`JSON.parse` with a reviver) 13.8 % self time; Flight serialisation ≈ 8 %; re-embedding the payload as `self.__next_f.push` ≈ 3 %; `Intl.DateTimeFormat` construction in the app's date helpers ≈ 8 %; GC 5 %; zod config parse ≈ 2 %. **The inline RSC payload was 82–84 % of every guest page** (106–113 KB of 125–137 KB) and 67 KB of it was one row: `ClerkProvider`'s appearance and Indonesian localisation, serialised from the root layout into pages whose visitors never sign in.
- **Decision:** no shell cache ships (it would add cross-guest risk for ≤ 1 ms). The two measured levers E-042 and E-043 ship instead. Evidence: `/vercel/share/work/logs/s21-bench-upper.json`, `s21-bench-b015.json`, `s21-bench-3arm.json` and the profile (sandbox-local, not retained); scratch `tools/template-harness/.out/s21/cpu-bench.mjs` (git-ignored).

### E-042 · B-015: ClerkProvider only around the signed-in route groups (s21)
- **Change:** `components/auth/auth-provider.tsx` (`ClerkProvider` with the unchanged appearance and localisation) wraps `app/(login)`, `app/(customer)` and `app/(admin)` through new group layouts; the root layout renders only the document (edited by a checked script, scaffold-copy hazard, diff confirmed). Every client-side Clerk import was already inside those groups; server-side `auth()` / `currentUser()` (`@clerk/nextjs/server`, used by `/preview` through `getUser`) needs no provider.
- **Tests:** `tests/b015-clerk-scope.test.ts` (4): root layout mounts no provider; each group layout wraps its pages; every `@clerk/nextjs` client import lives in a signed-in group; `app/i`, `app/preview` and `components/invitation` import nothing from Clerk.
- **Acceptance (s21, D-012 snapshots of the same data, before `xWS3KXzRmrlQhlvBsV-YW` vs after):** `b015-accept.mjs` **50 / 50**: ten guest pages (five layouts × full and portraits), five public pages and `/` carry no Clerk bootstrap in markup or RSC payload and load no JS chunk mentioning Clerk; `<main>` markup identical before and after with only build-specific server-action ids and the countdown's digits masked; `/sign-in` and `/sign-up` still mount the provider with appearance and localisation. Guest page **125,424 → 50,191 bytes**; RSC payload 94,587 → 26,608 characters.
- **Measured (same host, interleaved, 4 × 300 requests, concurrency 4, five layouts):** server CPU per guest view median **28.5 → 24.9 ms**; 37.6 → 45.6 req/s; p95 147 → 123 ms; mean response 131.5 → 56.3 KB.
- **Not verified:** sign-in, sign-up, dashboard and admin with real Clerk keys (P-INV-010). Those routes build and render their provider; a signed-in session was not exercised.
- **Risk / rollback:** M (a page relying on the root provider would break; the scan test guards it). Rollback: revert the four layout files and the provider component.

### E-043 · Shared date formatters (s21)
- **Change:** `lib/invitation/event-time.ts` keeps one `Intl.DateTimeFormat` per zone and format (three formats × four zones `resolve()` can return, ≤ 12 instances); `formatPlainDate` uses one module-level formatter. Instances hold only locale and options, so sharing them across requests shares no request data.
- **Tests:** `tests/s21-shared-formatters.test.ts` (2): every exported formatter equals the pre-s21 implementation (kept verbatim in the test) over 13 values × 6 fallback zones, twice, interleaved across zones; plain dates likewise.
- **Measured (same run as E-042, third arm):** server CPU median **24.9 → 22.2 ms** on top of E-042 (rounds 18.3–22.6 vs 22.0–25.9; one round overlaps); 45.6 → 56.2 req/s; p95 123 → 107 ms. Together with E-042: **28.5 ��� 22.2 ms (−22 %)**, every combined round below every baseline round; the acceptance above also passes 50 / 50 against this build (`NqzpKCeG8LELJLqtDZ24K`).
- **Against B-014's target** (≤ 2 × `next-min`, ≈ 8 ms on the s09 host): not met; the remaining cost is React's HTML render and Flight work proportional to the page.
- **Risk / rollback:** L. Revert two files.

### E-044 · Harness × product families: shell bytes vs family bytes, with a total guest-page budget (s21)
- **Change:** `tools/template-harness/product-bytes.ts` loads one guest page per family in headless Chrome (cache off, 360 × 800, until load + 1 s quiet) and records every request with its decoded and served bytes. **Shell** = every counted resource other than the document that every family page requests under the same (content-hashed) URL, counted once; **family** = the page's document (markup + inline RSC payload, shared blocks inside it included) plus anything not every family loads; fetches (RSC prefetches) and the favicon are listed, not counted. `PRODUCT_PAGE_BUDGETS` (`policy.ts`): shell ≤ 640 KiB, family ≤ 80 KiB, page ≤ 720 KiB decoded and ≤ 320 KiB as served (the basic tier's cold load anchored on C-INV-005). Internal and anchored on this build with stated headroom, not competitor parity. Exit 0 PASS / 1 FAIL / 3 INCOMPLETE like the other browser gates.
- **Why decoded bytes:** they are what the phone parses and do not depend on the host's compression; the served total keeps the cold-load budget comparable with C-INV-005 (lab `next start`, gzip).
- **Measured (the five `review-<layout>-full` pages, guest `budi-hartono`):** final build `NqzpKCeG8LELJLqtDZ24K`: shell **609 KiB** decoded (JS 464, CSS 91, fonts 54; 15 resources; 217 KiB served); families **49.0 / 53.9 / 56.4 / 60.7 / 54.8 KiB** (legacy / Gapura / Kartu / Riang / Lontar; RSC payload 30–37 KiB of each); pages 658–670 KiB decoded, 233–235 KiB served → **PASS**. Pre-B-015 build `xWS3KXzRmrlQhlvBsV-YW`, same data: shell 786 KiB (JS 641: Clerk's client code), families 123–134 KiB (payload 103–110 KiB), pages 909��920 KiB decoded, 300–302 KiB served → **FAIL** on shell, family and page total for all five. No family loads a resource of its own today (the family contract allows two shared self-hosted fonts and no family JS), so what tells families apart is their document: Riang's is the largest (+11.7 KiB over legacy).

## Session 23r (2026-11-06; recovery from `05235da` after the lost s23 sandbox)
Baseline reproduced: typecheck 0; tests 279 / 278 / 1 (the E-047 assertion); migrations 0000–0015; `verify-payment` 10 / 10; `verify-view-dedupe` 6 / 6. **The lost s23 log is context only**: none of its code exists, and none of its figures are reported here.

### E-047 completed · D-040: children's photos behind an access contract (s23r)
- **Contract (D-040):**
  - *Storage:* new child-photo uploads (`purpose=child-photo` from the aqiqah, khitanan and birthday fields only) are stored as `protected/<invitation>/<24 hex>.<ext>`; every other upload keeps `<invitation>/<24 hex>.<ext>`.
  - *Delivery:* `/media/protected/*` serves the invitation's signed-in owner only (`private, no-store`, `noindex`, sandbox CSP); an administrator's session, no session, a missing middleware or any error in the check yields a private 404 before any read (`lib/storage/serve-media.ts`). The audience is decided on the joined key, so `/media/protected%2F…` (one decoded segment) cannot reach the public branch. Public `/media/*` is unchanged.
  - *Guests:* `/i/<slug>/<guest>/photo/<slot>` (`lib/invitation/child-photo-delivery.ts`) re-checks on every request: non-draft invitation, the B-011 link rule, the event type's slot, the host's switch, and an upload of **this** invitation (protected, or public from before D-040); `private, no-store`, `noindex`, `no-referrer`. External URLs are never proxied.
  - *Render:* `childPhotoSrcFor` (`portraits.ts`): personal pages carry only the photo route; the preview keeps the stored path (owner session); another invitation's upload or an avatar renders nothing, on personal pages and in the preview; a protected path never renders in a public field (`safeImageSrc` refuses it), including a tasyakuran (adult) profile.
  - *Save:* the schema accepts a protected path in `birthdayPerson.photoUrl` and `ceremonyProfile.photoUrl` only; `updateDetailConfig` refuses (400 `INVALID_MEDIA`, field paths only) a protected path outside this event type's child field or naming another invitation, and a child photo naming another invitation's upload or an avatar. Only sections the save touches are checked, so a stored pre-D-040 value never blocks other sections.
  - *Middleware:* `middleware.ts` matches `/media/protected/(.*)` explicitly (its general matcher skips image extensions, so Clerk's session would otherwise be absent) and returns `NextResponse.next()` there without page CSP or locale cookies.
- **Migration:** `scripts/migrate-child-photos.ts` (dry run by default; `--apply`, `--delete-public`, `--rollback`, `--invitation`). Child-photo field of the live config and every saved version; copy → read back → SHA-256 compare before any row changes; one guarded transaction per path; repeat runs are no-ops; a public object is removed only when no live config, saved version, gallery row or avatar of any invitation still names it; rollback restores public objects and paths and keeps the protected copy.
- **Deployment order:** (1) deploy the code (it reads both namespaces); (2) `migrate-child-photos.ts` dry run, then `--apply`; (3) after checking pages, `--apply --delete-public`. **Rollback:** `--rollback --apply` first (restores public objects and paths), then revert the code; the pre-D-040 schema would refuse protected paths and the old `/media` would serve none. With S3 the bucket must not grant public reads (reads are proxied through `/media`). Bytes already served publicly (browser caches up to a year, saved copies) cannot be recalled; a forwarded personal link keeps working for whoever holds it (the link is the capability).
- **Tests (new in s23r):** `tests/e047-child-photo-access.test.ts` (13): path rules; `/media` audience incl. encoded slashes; public delivery unchanged; protected delivery to the owner and private 404s for no session, missing middleware and database errors with nothing read; the photo route's 200s and 14 refusals (slot, draft, unknown / deleted / rotated guest, switch off and explicitly off, other event types, other invitation's protected and public uploads, avatar, external URL, malformed path, missing object); B-011 token links; the render rule; every layout × aqiqah / khitanan / birthday (personal page = route only, public page = nothing, preview = stored path, cross-invitation = nothing); schema placement; save-time ownership; wiring (middleware matcher, upload purpose, editor fields, admin refusal). `tests/d038-portraits.test.ts:97` now asserts the personal photo route **and** the absence of the storage path (stricter than before). Suite **291 / 291**, typecheck 0.
- **Migration acceptance (s23r, scratch Postgres, synthetic rows, scratch storage root):** `scripts/verify-child-photo-migration.ts` **23 / 23** (dry run writes nothing; bytes verified; live config and saved versions follow; other keys preserved; foreign, external and adult photos untouched; missing object reported without a write; repeat is a no-op; delete-public removes only the unreferenced object and keeps one named by another invitation and one used as a cover; rollback restores bytes and paths; re-apply reuses copies).
- **Not verified:** a signed-in owner reading `/media/protected/*` and the editor thumbnail of a protected upload (real Clerk keys, P-INV-010: **EXT**).
- **Risk / rollback:** M (upload, render, delivery and save paths). Rollback as above.

### E-053 · Q-O11 resolved: the family budget judges design bytes; host uploads stay in the page totals (s25; harness only)
- **Decision:** the owner delegated Q-O11 on 2026-11-06 (s25) and named a preferred direction. Recorded as Q-D17 (reversible). The limits (640 / 80 / 720 / 320 KiB) are unchanged; only what the family budget measures changed.
- **Change (tooling, no product code):**
  - `policy.ts`: `FAMILY_BUDGET_BASIS = 'design'`.
  - `checkProductBudgets(attribution, budgets, basis)` judges the family budget on `designBytes` (`design`) or `familyBytes` (`family`, the s21–s24 basis). The shell and both page totals are the same on either basis and always contain the host uploads.
  - `product-bytes.ts`: `--family-basis design|family` overrides the basis for one run (an invalid value exits 2 before Chrome starts).
  - `product-bytes.json` adds `familyBudgetBasis`, `otherBasis` (the other basis's verdict and reasons; it never sets the exit code) and `hostUploads` (per family: count, decoded and transfer bytes). `attribution` and `pages` keep every resource and the family figure with the uploads.
  - The console prints host uploads per layout on their own line. It flags layouts that fetch different uploads for the same content, a layout choice that only the page totals limit. When uploads exist, it also prints the other basis's verdict.

  - Host uploads are reported per layout, and layouts that fetch different uploads are flagged (the extra upload fails the page total, not the family budget).
  - The basis default and the one-run override.
  
  The s24 test now asserts both bases, and two existing assertions take the new `design bytes` label.

## Session 26 (2026-11-06; baseline `2a6015f`; upgrade phase U1 under ADR-O11 after both gates passed, ADR-O12)

### E-054 · B-041: server-side theme entitlement on config writes (s26, U1)
- **Behaviour before (confirmed in code at `2a6015f`):**
  - `updateDetailConfig` stored any positive `theme.templateId` and any palette text.
  - Checkout charges by the tier of `invitations.template_id`, but guests see `config.theme.paletteId`.
  - So a Standard invitation could render a Premium palette, before or after paying. Only publishing asked the orders table.
- **Acceptance (U1 exit):** a published Standard invitation cannot switch to a Premium theme without an order; a refused write changes nothing; stored configs keep saving.
- **Change (D-042):**
  - New `lib/invitation/theme-entitlement.ts` (pure): `paletteTier`, `storedThemeFields`, `changedThemeFields`, `needsTierLookup`, `themeChangeIssues`, `ThemeEntitlementError`.
    - A palette is Premium when no free catalog theme uses it; the fallback `ivory` is always Standard.
    - Unregistered palettes are refused, and `theme.templateId` is pinned to the invitation's catalog theme.
    - Only changed fields are checked.
  - `lib/invitation/service.ts`: `updateDetailConfig` checks changed theme fields before its transaction. `themeTier` (Premium theme, or a PAID Premium order) is queried only for a Premium palette.
  - `app/api/invitations/[id]/detail/route.ts`: 403 `THEME_NOT_ENTITLED` with paths and reasons only.
  - `docs/systems/payment.md`: new section "Entitlement tema saat menyimpan".
- **Tests:**
  - `tests/b041-theme-entitlement.test.ts`, 13 / 13: the palette classification over the whole catalog (today's Premium set pinned), the fallback, unknown ids, Standard vs Premium, unchanged and cleared fields, the theme pin, lenient stored reads, the error text, and the writer and route wiring.
  - `scripts/verify-theme-entitlement.ts` (database), **14 / 14** on a scratch database:
    - a real catalog invitation published by a settled Standard order refuses `midnight` (config and versions untouched), another catalog theme, and an unknown palette;
    - it still saves a layout change and another Standard palette;
    - after a settled Premium order, `midnight` is accepted;
    - an unpaid Standard draft refuses `gold-noir`; a Premium draft accepts it.
    - It leaves 0 rows behind.
  - **Negative control:** with the check removed in a throw-away copy, the same script reports 7 / 14 FAIL, exactly the leak.
- **Checks:**
  - Typecheck 0.
  - CI database job (`scripts/ci/local.sh database`): 30 / 30 (payment 10, view-dedupe 6, theme entitlement 14).
- **Limits:**
  - The editor's theme tab never offered palette changes; the check closes the API path, which is where the leak was.
  - Versions restored from before s26 may carry a palette the rule would now refuse. A restore goes through the same writer, so the check applies.
  - The feature matrix's own column (FM-106) still describes `2a6015f` until the next column switch.
- **Risk / rollback:** a host with a legitimately Premium look keeps it (unchanged fields pass). Revert the module, the service block and the route mapping; there is no schema change.

### E-055 · B-020 CI: the required checks, the B-012 product gates, research checks and the Rust suite on every push (s26, U1)
- **Behaviour before:** no CI workflow. The B-012 gates, `verify-payment` and `verify-view-dedupe` ran only by hand (B-020 PARTIAL, B-012 PARTIAL).
- **Change:**
  - [historical GitHub workflow](https://github.com/faznode/invitesion/blob/cf052522115763ef32ae4ffafd3dbeebb2ae5296/.github/workflows/ci.yml), five jobs, each a script in `scripts/ci/`:
