### E-056 · F-01: Next.js 16.4.5 stable adopted (s27; investigated under U1, adopted at the start of U2)
- **Behaviour before (code at `87e14dc`):** `next 15.6.0-canary.59`, React 19.1.4; `middleware.ts` (deprecated convention, AUDIT-2026-07-27 B4); `experimental.ppr: 'incremental'` with per-segment `experimental_ppr`; `experimental.clientSegmentCache`. *Reported (owner, s26):* a Next 16.4.5 copy passed 324 tests and built; a follow-up `middleware.ts` → `proxy.ts` copy built with an invalid Proxy runtime configuration and FAILED; nothing was adopted.
- **Releases and migration rules (official sources, checked s27):** npm registry dist-tags: `next` latest 16.4.5 (canary 16.5.0-canary.42, backport 15.5.29); `react` / `react-dom` latest 19.3.1; `@types/react` / `@types/react-dom` 19.3.0; `next@16.4.5` accepts React `^19.0.0`. Next.js "How to upgrade to version 16" (nextjs.org/docs/app/guides/upgrading/version-16): `middleware` becomes `proxy`, Proxy runs on Node.js and takes no runtime config, `experimental.ppr` is removed (Cache Components replace it), Turbopack is the default.
- **Acceptance (06 F-01):** typecheck, tests, build, harness gates and payment / view-dedupe checks pass; no guest-page regression beyond run variation; verified by CI plus the same-host interleaved bench. Additionally (brief): real 404s, protected-media coverage, authorisation, locale behaviour and Clerk-free guest pages preserved; no Cache Components; no weakened assertion.
- **Change:**
  - `package.json` / `pnpm-lock.yaml`: next 16.4.5, react / react-dom 19.3.1, @types/react / @types/react-dom 19.3.0 (exact pins); `dev` is `next dev`. Clerk unchanged.
  - `middleware.ts` → `proxy.ts` with the handler unchanged; the `runtime: 'nodejs'` key removed (the cause of the s26 build failure).
  - `next.config.ts`: no `experimental` block (ppr removed; the client segment cache is Next 16's default router); no `cacheComponents`, whose prerendered shell would flush a 200 before `notFound()` (B-011, QA-003); `agentRules: false` (below).
  - `app/layout.tsx` and both guest pages: `experimental_ppr` exports removed; the guest pages stay `force-dynamic`.
  - `lib/storage/index.ts`: `/*turbopackIgnore: true*/` on the env-dependent upload root, so Next 16's file tracing does not copy the project into the server output.
  - `local-stack.sh`, `scripts/ci/app.sh`, `scripts/ci/lib.sh`: the measurement variant sets aside either file name; labels.
  - Tests rewritten to the new facts, not loosened: `content-contract` (locale cookie in `proxy.ts`); `e047-child-photo-access` (the proxy covers `/media/protected/*`, `middleware.ts` absent, no `runtime` key); `p0-guest-features` (B-011's real 404 now rests on: no `ppr` / `cacheComponents`, no `experimental_ppr` or `<Suspense>` in the layout or guest pages, `force-dynamic` and `notFound()` on both pages, no `loading.tsx` above them). New `tests/f01-next-stable.test.ts` (3: exact stable pins, `agentRules`, one interception file).
  - `README.md` product-code row. AUDIT-2026-07-27 B4 is resolved by this entry.
- **Found during adoption:** the first `next dev` of 16.4.5 appended a "nextjs-agent-rules" instruction block to the owner-authored `AGENTS.md` (18:37Z). Restored byte-identical from `87e14dc`; `agentRules: false` (documented option, `node_modules/next/dist/docs/…/agentRules.md`) stops it and removes such a block; `AGENTS.md` stayed identical across the next dev restart. Negative control: with the option removed and the block present, the new test fails (1 / 3).
- **Evidence:**
  - Copy, app job: typecheck 0, **324 / 324**, production build with `proxy.ts` (`ƒ Proxy (Middleware)`). Baseline `87e14dc`, same job: 324 / 324, build with middleware.
  - Copy, product gates (Next 16 measurement build): real 404 × 3; wedding and programme bytes PASS; design acceptance **174 / 174**; cover fold **90 / 90**; rendered contrast **9 / 9**. Same verdicts as the baseline run on `87e14dc` in this session.
  - Copy, database job: **30 / 30** (payment 10, view-dedupe 6, theme entitlement 14).
  - Same-host interleaved bench, 12 + 12 Lighthouse runs (`../04-template-benchmarks/own-builds/own-build-comparison-s27-f01.md`): transfer **−3,768 B** in every run (document 12.4 → 9.2 KB, one request fewer; exact p 7.4 × 10⁻⁷); FCP, LCP, SI, TBT, TTI, CLS and score do not separate (p 0.29–1.0). LCP is not established either way (different clusters, means 2.39 vs 2.34 s).
  - Deliverable (final tree with E-057 / E-058): see the closing check run after E-058.
- **Decision: ADOPT.** Every acceptance criterion holds; the only separated metric improved.
- **Limits:** measurement builds run without the interception file (D-012: placeholder Clerk keys), so the proxy's CSP, locale cookie and Clerk session are verified by build, tests and code, not served; a real-key run belongs to DA-01 (deferred, NOT RUN). Clerk 7 on Next 16 is proven to build, not to sign in. The platform preview has no database (guest routes answer 500 `POSTGRES_URL is not set` there, before and after).
- **Risk / rollback:** revert the upgrade (package.json, lockfile, `proxy.ts` back to `middleware.ts` with its runtime key, `next.config.ts`, the pages and tests); no data change.

### E-057 �� R-01: the Rust API foundation in `services/api` (s27, U2)
- **Behaviour before:** no service, contract pipeline or telemetry (06 R-01); only the isolated s26 experiment.
- **Acceptance (06 R-01):** CI builds and tests it on a migrated Postgres; the OpenAPI drift check fails on change; the preview deployment answers health within SLO; cold start recorded; trace visible.
- **Change (reviewed against the experiment's limits before promotion):**
  - `services/api` (axum 0.8.9, sqlx 0.9 with rustls, utoipa 6, jsonwebtoken 11, Rust 1.99, edition 2024): `GET /v1/health` (liveness, unsigned), `GET /v1/health/ready` (signed; one Postgres round trip, else 503 `database.unavailable`), `GET /v1/me` (signed + owner token), `POST /v1/views` (E-058).
  - RFC 9457 problem documents with a stable `code` (`domain.reason`) for every error, including a 10 s request timeout (`request.timeout`); `x-request-id` taken from Next.js or minted, on every response.
  - Internal service signature: `x-invitesion-signature: t=<unix>,v1=<HMAC-SHA256 of t.METHOD.path_and_query.sha256(body)>`, 60 s skew, two secrets during a rotation, secrets ≥ 32 bytes, bodies bounded to 64 KiB and verified before any extractor.
  - Owner tokens: RS256 only, against the issuer's JWKS (cached; refetched on an unknown `kid` at most once per interval); issuer, `exp` and `nbf` with 5 s leeway; `azp` among the allowed origins; `sub` → `users.clerk_user_id` (not deleted), role from the database, never from the token; without configuration owner endpoints answer 503.
  - Contract pipeline `scripts/api/contract.sh generate|check`: `services/api/openapi.json` (OpenAPI 3.1 from the code), `lib/api/schema.d.ts` (openapi-typescript **7.13.0**, exact devDependency), `.sqlx` offline query metadata described against a drizzle-migrated database. drizzle-kit stays the only migration owner; the service runs no DDL.
  - Next.js side: `lib/api/signature.ts`, `lib/api/client.ts` (typed by the generated schema, signs exactly the bytes it sends, 3 s timeout, `ApiProblem` with stable codes, fails closed without `API_BASE_URL` / a 32-character `INTERNAL_API_SECRET`).
  - Shared vectors `contracts/vectors/service-signature.json` (expected values from an independent implementation) run by `tests/api-contract.test.ts` and `services/api/tests/contract_vectors.rs`.
  - Telemetry (G-020): one JSON log line per request (request id, matched route, method, status, latency).
  - Deployment files: `services/api/vercel.json` (every path to `api/index.rs`, region `sin1`; Vercel's Rust runtime is beta on Fluid compute), container fallback `src/main.rs`.
  - CI (`scripts/ci/rust.sh`, job name unchanged so a required check keeps matching): fmt; clippy with `SQLX_OFFLINE=true` and `-D warnings`; tests; the contract check; the views slice end to end; Swatinem cache covers `services/api`. `.gitignore`: generated Rust build directories (ignored, not repository content). `.env.example`: the Next.js-side variables.
- **Evidence (deliverable, `local.sh rust`):** fmt and clippy clean; **20 / 20** Rust tests (signature vectors 3, database 3, HTTP contract 8, owner tokens 6); `contract.sh check` current; feasibility 17 / 17. TypeScript: `tests/api-contract.test.ts` 7 / 7 inside the app job. Drift negative control (scratch copy): a mutated `openapi.json` and a mutated `schema.d.ts` each fail the check (exit 1, both named).
- **Not done (tracked):** OpenTelemetry trace export (spans exist in the JSON logs; export needs a collector endpoint, an operational dependency); the preview deployment, its health SLO and cold-start figures (owner steps: a Vercel project with root `services/api`, `INTERNAL_API_SECRET`, database URL); the first GitHub Actions run of the extended job (agents never push).

### E-059 · R-03: RSVP behind `BACKEND_RSVP`, exactly one writer, keyed retries and shared limits (code s27, completed s28, U2)
- **Behaviour before (code at `87e14dc`):** guests' RSVPs and hosts' overrides were written only by the Next.js server actions, with no write limit (B-013) and no idempotency.
- **Found at `f935d39` (s27 code, in no record until s28):**
  - Shared vectors `contracts/vectors/rsvp-rules.json` and `rsvp-decisions.json`; the pure decision `lib/invitation/rsvp-decision.ts` (`decideGuestRsvp`, `decideHostRsvp`) used by both actions; `tests/rsvp-vectors.test.ts`, `tests/rsvp-decisions.test.ts`.
  - The Rust port: lenient config read, rules and decision (`services/api/src/rsvp/`), `POST /v1/guest-links/{slug}/{guest}/rsvp` and `POST /v1/invitations/{invitation}/guests/{guest}/rsvp-override` (row lock, one UPDATE plus the RSVP analytics row per transaction), `Idempotency-Key` (`idempotency.rs`), per-link and per-address fixed-window limits (`limits.rs`), owner tokens resolved to `users` with administrators refused; `tests/rsvp_vectors.rs` 4, `tests/rsvp_database.rs` 3.
  - Additive drizzle migration 0016 (`api_idempotency_keys`, `api_rate_limits`); regenerated OpenAPI, TypeScript types and `.sqlx`.
  - Next.js: `BACKEND_RSVP` in `lib/backend.ts`, `lib/invitation/rsvp-writer.ts`, both call sites, `ownerSessionToken()` forwarding, a form key minted per rendering of the personal page, the "busy" notice.
- **Gaps found in s28 (review of that code before writing any):**
  1. No acceptance through the Next.js call sites, the typed client and a running binary (views had `verify-views-api.ts`), and the API-client tests predated the templated RSVP calls.
  2. A transport failure or 5xx threw (a generic error page) with no retry, and the dashboard sent no key, so an answer lost after a commit could only be re-sent as a second write.
  3. The per-address limit read the leftmost `X-Forwarded-For` entry or `X-Real-IP`, both client-controlled behind a proxy that appends, and keyed IPv6 per address (a /64 rotates freely).
  4. The owner check's 403 was an error page; `Retry-After` was parsed and dropped.
  5. `lib/db/schema.ts` cited D-042 (the theme-entitlement decision) for 0016; the records still said "R-03 OPEN".
- **Acceptance (06 R-03, plus the s28 brief):** every existing RSVP scenario passes against the API with stored shapes unchanged; the vectors pass in both languages; a burst above the limit gets 429 problems; exactly one writer per selection, nothing written when the Rust writer fails; paths, encoding and signatures over the bytes sent; owner tokens, ownership and role; form keys, duplicates, changed bodies and transport ambiguity; the 403 / 404 / 409 / 422 / 429 and transport mapping to the intended notices with the event, question and retry time; deadlines, maybe, allowances, per-event answers, eligibility, questions and malformed stored sections; concurrency, replays and limit boundaries; the trusted-proxy contract.
- **Change (s28; D-043):**
  - `lib/invitation/rsvp-writer.ts`: every write carries a key (the form's, else minted; one per host override); an unknown outcome (status 0 or 5xx) is sent exactly once more under the same key, then reported `unconfirmed` (guest: with the key); `busy` carries `retryAfter`; the owner check's 403 → `signin`; 400 / 401 propagate. Unconfirmed outcomes log status and code only.
  - `lib/net/client-address.ts` (new): the trusted-proxy contract (`TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS`, default 1, 0 trusts none; only IP literals; IPv6 zone ids refused). `app/i/[slug]/actions.ts` reads the address only through it.
  - `lib/invitation/rsvp-form-key.ts` (new): the form-key pattern and `rsvpFormKey()`; the page reuses a carried key only after `?rsvp=retry`.
  - Notices: `?rsvp=busy&rsvpr=<s>` ("coba lagi dalam N menit"), `?rsvp=retry&rsvpk=<key>` ("belum bisa dipastikan tersimpan … jawaban yang sama tidak akan tercatat dua kali"); the dashboard says `?error=unconfirmed` in its own words (`role="alert"`) and sends a refused session to sign-in.
  - `services/api/src/limits.rs` `address_key`: IPv4 and IPv4-mapped by address, IPv6 by /64; anything else skips the per-address limit with a log line that carries no value. Contract regenerated (the `clientIp` description only; `.sqlx` unchanged).
  - `scripts/verify-rsvp-api.ts` (new): the real server actions with only `redirect` / `revalidatePath` / `headers` and Clerk's session stubbed, against the running binary and the database; an RS256 JWKS served by the script (one key pair per API instance; no Clerk credential). `scripts/ci/rust.sh` starts the binary once (owner tokens; limits 8 / 20 per 600 s) for both slices and adds the step "rsvp end to end".
  - Records: D-043; `services/api/README.md`, `.env.example` (`BACKEND_RSVP`, `TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS`), `docs/systems/guest-management.md`, `docs/DATABASE_SCHEMA.md` (0016), the `AGENTS.md` stack block, the schema comment (D-043).
- **Tests (app job):** `tests/rsvp-writer.test.ts` 11 (retry under the same key with identical bytes, unconfirmed, key reuse, every problem mapping, deployment errors without a retry, segments refused before a request, host mapping, call-site shape), `tests/client-address.test.ts` 4, `tests/rsvp-form-key.test.ts` 2, `tests/api-contract.test.ts` +4 (templated paths and their signature, problem extras, `BACKEND_RSVP`). Rust: `address_keys_group_an_ipv6_line_by_its_64_prefix`.
- **Evidence (`verify-rsvp-api`, 24 / 24 on the final tree, in CI and twice back to back against one binary):**
  - **34 / 34** guest scenarios give the same notice, guest row (every column outside identity) and analytics row through both writers: clamped, missing, unreadable and zero counts, allowance 1, decline, maybe and maybe off, unknown and missing status, per-event (both, one missing, maybe, a deleted event's answer kept, flag off, single answer replacing), invite-only (not invited, invited, partly answered, flag off), questions (trimmed, conditional, several values, missing, unoffered option, decline clearing, flag off), deadline, switched off, expired by date and status, draft, malformed stored sections and event answers, a broken stored question.
  - Links: a tokened guest is refused by slug, an unknown link is closed, a token with `-` and `_` writes. 14 slugs and segments needing encoding (`/`, `?`, `#`, `%`, `%2e%2e`, spaces, `ñ`, 300 characters, backslashes, a path-injection attempt) are refused as closed with no write and no signature error.
  - One writer: under `rust` with the API down, "unconfirmed" with the form's key and nothing written; an API answering 503 is asked exactly twice under one key with the same signed path and bytes; under `next` the API is never called.
  - Ambiguity: the API commits and the answer is lost → the retry replays (`Idempotent-Replayed: true`), one write; both answers lost → "unconfirmed", and re-sending under the carried key replays (still one write); the carried key with a changed answer writes once more.
  - Keys: 6 concurrent sends of one form → all "saved", one write, one stored key; an invalid form key is ignored and the writer mints one.
  - Limits: per link, 8 pass and the 9th is "busy" with `rsvpr` in 1…600 and writes nothing, and after the window it writes again; per address, 20 writes over 3 links pass and a client-supplied left entry does not escape; `TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS=0` applies no per-address limit; 20 IPv6 addresses of one /64 share one bucket (21 hits) while the next /64 has its own; the table holds hashes only.
  - Host: **8 / 8** dashboard operations give the same result, row and analytics row through both writers (including `event:<id>:…`, an unknown event and an unknown status); concurrent overrides of two events lose neither answer; another customer's token and a foreign guest are "not found", an administrator and an unknown account sign in, expired / foreign-issuer / foreign-party / unknown-key / forged tokens are 401 and propagate, and none writes; a session without a token signs in; the API down is `?error=unconfirmed` with nothing written; `next` needs no API.
  - **Negative controls** (scratch copy, same binary): M1 the TypeScript writer's analytics meta changed → parity 13 / 34 (the 21 writing scenarios fail); M2 the retry under a fresh key ��� 4 checks fail (two writes after a lost answer); M3 `BACKEND_RSVP=rust` ignored → 13 checks fail; M4 the leftmost forwarded entry read → only the forged-address check fails; M5 the owner check's 403 mapped to "not found" → only the ownership / role check fails.
  - **Gates on the final tree:** app typecheck 0, **363 / 363**, build with proxy; database PASS; rust fmt, clippy, **37 / 37**, contract current, views 9 / 9, **RSVP 24 / 24**, feasibility 17 / 17; research PASS after this entry (its reference check had caught D-043 citing E-059 before it existed); product gates in the session file.
- **Limits:**
  - The owner-token chain is proven with locally minted RS256 tokens served as a JWKS; Clerk's own session tokens (`getToken()` → issuer, `azp`) are DA-01 (deferred, NOT RUN).
  - Concurrent sends of one form each pass the limiter before one commits, so at the boundary a twin can see "busy" while the other saved (D-043). A dashboard double click is two overrides with one result.
  - The per-address limit is as good as the deployment's proxy contract.
- **Not done (operational, tracked):** the preview deployment with `OWNER_TOKEN_*`, `BACKEND_RSVP=rust` on preview under load, 7 days within the error budget, then removal of the TypeScript writer (U2 exit). Owner steps.
- **Risk / rollback:** `BACKEND_RSVP=next` (the default) at any time; both writers write the same columns in the same shapes. 0016's tables stay (additive, the API's own).

### E-060 · R-04: wishes with moderation, replies and visibility behind `BACKEND_WISHES` (s28, U2)
- **Behaviour before (code at `f935d39`):** the guest action inserted every wish visible with no limit. The loader already read only `is_hidden = false` (newest 50 and a count), but nothing ever set the flag, so every wish was listed and counted, through no index. Hosts had no moderation, deletion or reply (B-028, B-013, B-017). The guestbook tab warned that moderation did not work yet.
- **Acceptance (06 R-04, plus the s28 brief):** hidden wishes never render, and replies do. Limits answer 429. The guest-page query uses the new index (`EXPLAIN`). Beyond that: moderation and visibility, guest and host authorisation, the additive index through drizzle-kit, shared behavioural contracts, the API integration, writer selection with rollback, exactly one writer per call with no write after an ambiguous failure, and R-03's standards for keys, encoding, mapping and concurrency.
- **Change (s28; D-044):**
  - Schema: migration **0017** (drizzle-kit, additive): `guest_messages.reviewed_at`, `host_reply`, `replied_at`, index `guest_messages_invitation_visible_idx (invitation_id, is_hidden, created_at)`. `lib/db/schema.ts` documents the three states.
  - Config: `guestbook.moderation` (boolean, default false) and `guestbook.visibility` (`public` | `host`, absent = public) in `lib/invitation/detail-config.ts`. The editor's guestbook tab has two toggles and a pointer to the Wishes page. The content contract drops its "moderation does not work" warning.
  - Rules: `lib/invitation/wish-decision.ts` (`guestbookRules`, `decideGuestWish`, `decideHostReply`, `moderationUpdate`, `wishState`). Shared vectors `contracts/vectors/wish-decisions.json`: the lenient read, guest decisions, replies, states and moderation.
  - Guest side: `submitGuestMessage` uses the decision (TypeScript writer) or `writeGuestWishThroughApi` (`lib/invitation/wish-writer.ts`) under `BACKEND_WISHES=rust`, never both. The form carries a per-render key (the RSVP form-key helper, reused only after `?wish=retry`). Notices: `saved`, `pending` ("akan tampil setelah disetujui tuan rumah"), "delivered to the hosts" on a hosts-only guestbook, `invalid`, `closed`, `busy` with minutes, `retry`. The form says when wishes wait for approval or are read only by the hosts.
  - Read side: `lib/invitation/public.ts` lists the newest 50 visible wishes with `hostReply` and counts visible wishes only. Both builders are exported so the acceptance can `EXPLAIN` the exact statements. A closed or hosts-only guestbook runs neither query.
  - Host side: new `/dashboard/invitations/[id]/wishes` (page and actions): filters all / pending / visible / hidden with counts; show (approve or restore), hide, delete, reply or clear. Owner only, administrators sign in, the wish must be the invitation's. Under `rust` the actions forward the owner token and map 403 to sign-in, `invitation_or_wish.not_found` to "not found", unknown outcomes to `?error=unconfirmed`. The manage center links the page.
  - Rust: `services/api/src/wishes/` (`content` lenient read, `decision`, `http`), the shared `guest_link.rs` (link resolution for RSVP and wishes), three endpoints (guest wish; host moderation; host reply) with one transaction each, keys, limits `RATE_LIMIT_WISH_LINK` (5/600) and `RATE_LIMIT_WISH_IP` (60/600), problems `wish.*` and `invitation_or_wish.not_found`. OpenAPI, `lib/api/schema.d.ts` and `.sqlx` regenerated (`contract.sh check` current).
  - `scripts/verify-wishes-api.ts` (new) and the step "wishes end to end" in `scripts/ci/rust.sh`. The binary gets wish limits of 8 / 20 per 600 s. The RSVP and wishes scripts share one owner-token key pair per API instance, because the API refetches its JWKS for an unknown `kid` at most every 30 s.
- **Tests:** app **+13**: `tests/wish-writer.test.ts` 6 (key and signed templated path, retry under the same key and bytes, minted and fresh keys, refusal mapping, host mapping, call-site shape), `tests/wish-decisions.test.ts` 5 (vector coverage, read, guest, reply, state), `tests/api-contract.test.ts` +2 (`BACKEND_WISHES`, wish paths). Rust **+7**: `tests/wish_vectors.rs` 4, `tests/wish_database.rs` 3 (the TypeScript writer's rows, one form = one wish under concurrency, limit boundaries).
- **Evidence (`verify-wishes-api`, 27 / 27, twice back to back against one binary at 21:15Z and again in the rust job):**
  - **23 / 23** guest scenarios give the same notice, wish row and analytics row through both writers. Covered: open, absent section, moderated, hosts-only, both, switched off (also before judging the message), broken section (lenient read), empty, JavaScript whitespace, NEL alone, inner line breaks, 1,000 / 1,001 units, 500 / 501 emoji, NUL, draft, expired by status and by date, unknown link, a tokened guest by slug (B-011), and a token with `-` and `_`.
  - 13 segments needing encoding are "closed" in either position through both writers, with no write and no signature error.
  - One writer: under `rust` with the API down, "unconfirmed" with the form's key and nothing written. A 503 is asked exactly twice under one key with identical signed path and bytes. Under `next` the API is never called.
  - Ambiguity: a commit whose answer is lost replays (`Idempotent-Replayed: true`) with one wish stored. Both answers lost gives "unconfirmed", and re-sending under the carried key replays. The carried key with a changed message stores one new wish.
  - Keys: 6 concurrent sends of one form are all "saved", with one wish, one analytics row and one stored key. An invalid form key is ignored and a key minted.
  - Limits: per link, 8 pass and the 9th is "busy" (`wishr` inside the window) with nothing written. Per address, 20 over 3 links pass, the next is busy, and a forged left entry does not escape. IPv6: 20 addresses of one /64 share a bucket, while the next /64 has its own. The limiter table holds keyed hashes only.
  - Host: **11 / 11** operations give the same answer and row through both writers (approve, hide, restore, hide a pending wish, delete, reply, clear, 1,001-unit reply, unknown action, another invitation's wish, a missing wish), and a foreign wish is untouched. Another customer's token is "not found". An administrator or unknown account signs in. Expired, foreign-issuer, foreign-party, unknown-key and forged tokens are 401 and propagate. No refusal changes the wish. A session without a token signs in. With the API down the result is "unconfirmed" with nothing written. A delete whose answer is lost replays instead of answering "not found".
  - Render: the loader lists the visible wish with its reply and counts visible wishes only. The rendered guestbook (`renderToStaticMarkup`) shows the visible wish and the reply, never a pending or hidden wish, and tells the guest about moderation. A hosts-only guestbook lists and renders no wish and says who reads them.
  - `EXPLAIN` on 40 fixture invitations × 100 wishes after `ANALYZE`: the guest page's list (`Limit` → index scan backward) and count (`Aggregate` → index-only scan) both use `guest_messages_invitation_visible_idx`. The fixture leaves no row behind.
  - **Negative controls** (scratch copy `/vercel/share/work/s28-mut`, same binary, runner in git-ignored `tools/template-harness/.out/s28/`): M1 the TypeScript writer ignores moderation → parity 21 / 23, 1 check fails. M2 the retry under a fresh key → 5 checks fail. M3 `BACKEND_WISHES=rust` ignored by the guest action → 10 checks fail. M4 the loader lists hidden wishes → the loader and render checks fail (2). M5 the owner check's 403 mapped to "not found" → only the ownership / role check fails.
  - RSVP after the R-04 refactor (shared `guest_link.rs`, form-key helper, key file): `verify-rsvp-api` **24 / 24** against the same binary.
- **Gates on the final tree (s28, 21:31–21:47Z, `scripts/ci/local.sh`):** app typecheck 0, **376 / 376**, build with proxy. Database PASS (payment, view dedupe, theme entitlement, schema drift). Rust fmt, clippy, **44 / 44**, contract current, views 9 / 9, RSVP 24 / 24, **wishes 27 / 27**, feasibility 17 / 17. Research PASS. Product gates: real 404 3 / 3, bytes within budgets (wedding family 38.3–48.8 KiB, programme 61.1–70.2 KiB, page ≤ 677.4 KiB decoded and ≤ 228.8 KiB served), design acceptance 174 / 174, cover fold 90 / 90, contrast 9 / 9.
- **Limits:**
  - Owner tokens are locally minted RS256 tokens, as in E-059. Clerk's own session tokens are DA-01 (deferred, NOT RUN).
  - The TypeScript writer applies no limits; it is the rollback path.
  - The dashboard page and the editor toggles are verified through their actions and a static render, not in a signed-in browser (DA-01).
- **Not done (operational, tracked):** `BACKEND_WISHES=rust` on preview with `OWNER_TOKEN_*`, under load, 7 days within the error budget, then removal of the TypeScript writer (U2 exit). Owner steps.
- **Risk / rollback:** `BACKEND_WISHES=next` (the default) at any time; both writers write the same columns in the same shapes. 0017 stays (additive). A host who switched moderation on keeps PENDING wishes hidden until approved, whichever writer is live.

### E-061 · B-009: invitation cover and background music (s28, U3; PARTIAL)

- **Implemented in the s28 export (D-045):** the guest renderer adds the Indonesian cover and one audio element when background music is enabled and has a supported URL. The cover can open before hydration or without JavaScript. The opening gesture can start playback, guests can open silently or use the pause/play control, reduced motion skips the transition, and form notices bypass the cover. The feature rolls back through `FLAG_GUEST_MUSIC=off`.
- **Verification recorded in s28:** 387 app tests and the production app build passed. The product byte census for the music fixture passed; the export adds the generated tone, review variants and the `cover-gate.ts` browser step to product-gates CI. The final B-009 browser result was not present in the s28 handoff. See `sessions/session-28-20261106-2016.md` for the R-03/R-04 baseline.
- **Transfer verification:** a fresh Chrome profile, a fresh document for each navigation, and deadlines for DevTools commands keep the browser harness from hanging between cases. The corrected harness passed **69 / 69 across nine pages** in Chrome for Testing against a local production measurement build and seeded PostgreSQL 16. The assertions and the 1.5 s acceptance limit are unchanged.
- **Open:** check the cover itself in rendered contrast and verify playback on a real mobile device/in-app browser with an owner-approved audio source before calling B-009 done. No new asset licence or live deployment is implied by the generated fixture.
- **Reconciled in s29 (from the owner's transfer record; *reported*, not re-run unless marked):**
  - Browser acceptance (`tools/template-harness/cover-gate.ts`, the product-gates step "cover gate and music") **69 / 69 across nine pages** in local Chrome for Testing against a production measurement build and seeded Postgres 16; the transfer operator repeated it twice with the same result.
  - Rendered contrast of the music cover **5 / 5** in local Chrome / Lighthouse, one page per layout (closes this entry's first open item as reported evidence).
  - Product checkpoint `cf05252`: **387 / 387** app tests, typecheck, production build, database checks, Rust checks, research checks and the product-page gates (design acceptance 174 / 174, cover fold 90 / 90, routine contrast 9 / 9, byte budgets PASS for the wedding, programme and music fixtures). Historical verification run: [Actions run 42311691842](https://github.com/faznode/invitesion/actions/runs/42311691842) on `cf05252`. `2c5fcde` then removed the workflow and recorded ADR-O13 with no product change.

## Session 29 (2026-11-07; baseline `2c5fcde`, tree `cb88795`; ADR-O13 sandbox checks, host replay isolation before any Rust rollout)
Every figure below was executed in s29 unless marked *reported*. Jobs ran through `scripts/ci/local.sh` (disposable copy, fresh database) with pnpm 10.34.9, Node 24.22.2, Rust 1.99.1, Postgres 16.15 and Chrome for Testing 158.0.8127.18. **Baseline on `2c5fcde` (rust job):** fmt, clippy, **44 / 44**, contract current, views end to end, RSVP 24 / 24, wishes 27 / 27, feasibility 17 / 17 (245 s).

### E-062 · Host replay isolation: a host's idempotency key bound to its owner, invitation and resource (s29, before any Rust rollout; D-046)
- **Behaviour before (`2c5fcde`):** `host_rsvp_override`, `moderate_wish` and `reply_to_wish` resolved the owner from the token and refused administrators. They then looked up the `Idempotency-Key` under `rsvp:host:<guest>`, `wish:host:<wish>` or `wish:reply:<wish>`, before checking that the invitation was the owner's. A recorded answer therefore replayed to any authenticated customer on any path that named the same guest or wish.
- **Reproduction (s29, before any change):**
  - `scripts/verify-host-replay-isolation.ts` ran against the `2c5fcde` binary built by the baseline rust job (sha256 `54ed7377…`). It used the real service signature, RS256 owner tokens served as a JWKS by the script, and chosen keys. Result: **15 FAILED, 14 passed.**
    - Each of the three operations failed four ways. Another customer reusing the owner's key with the same body got 200, replayed, with the cached answer (for a reply, the host's text). With a changed body they got 422 `idempotency.key_reused`. Under their own invitation, and the owner under another invitation, also got 200 replayed.
    - After a delete whose answer a proxy cut, all three foreign reuses got 200 replayed.
    - Passing: both tokens were valid and the owner's writes succeeded. No refusal changed a row, added an analytics row or recorded a key. The owner's own retries replayed, including the lost delete. The other customer's wish was untouched and no fixture row was left.
  - `services/api/tests/host_replay_isolation.rs` against `2c5fcde`'s handlers: 2 / 2 failed (12 isolation failures listed, plus the lost delete's foreign reuse). Administrators were already refused (403), because the role check precedes the lookup.
  - Logs stay outside tracked paths: `/vercel/share/work/logs/s29-replay-prefix.log`, `/vercel/share/work/logs/s29-replay-prefix-rust.log`.
- **Change (D-046):**
  - `services/api/src/idempotency.rs`: `host_scope(operation, owner, invitation, resource)`, with the unit test `host_scopes_bind_the_owner_the_invitation_and_the_resource`.
  - The three handlers build their scope with it (`rsvp:host`, `wish:host`, `wish:reply`). The order is unchanged: signature, path, owner and role, body, key, scope, lookup, then the locked ownership check and the write.
  - OpenAPI: the three `Idempotency-Key` descriptions state the binding. `services/api/openapi.json` and `lib/api/schema.d.ts` were regenerated (`bash scripts/api/contract.sh generate`); `.sqlx` is unchanged.
  - The acceptance `scripts/verify-host-replay-isolation.ts` (new) runs as the step "host replay isolation" in `scripts/ci/rust.sh`. The Rust test `services/api/tests/host_replay_isolation.rs` is new.
  - Cleanups that named the old host scopes now delete by owner (`scripts/verify-rsvp-api.ts`, `scripts/verify-wishes-api.ts`, `services/api/tests/rsvp_database.rs`). Both scripts' "no row behind" checks now count idempotency keys too.
  - Docs: `docs/DATABASE_SCHEMA.md` (scope formats and who may receive a replay) and the schema comment in `lib/db/schema.ts`.
- **Evidence after the change (s29, `scripts/ci/local.sh rust`, disposable copy and database, 60 s):** fmt and clippy clean, **47 / 47** service tests (+1 unit, +2 database), contract current, views end to end, RSVP **24 / 24**, wishes **27 / 27**, **host replay isolation 29 / 29**, feasibility 17 / 17.
  - Every refusal is a 404 (`invitation_or_guest.not_found` or `invitation_or_wish.not_found`; administrators 403 `owner.role_not_allowed`), with no replay header and no cached answer.
  - Rows, analytics rows and the key table are unchanged by refusals, and the owner's retries replay, including the lost delete.
- **Limits:** keys recorded under the old scopes stop replaying when this deploys; with no Rust writer live, no retry is affected. The proof uses locally minted RS256 tokens; Clerk's own session tokens are DA-01 (deferred, NOT RUN).
- **Risk / rollback:** no stored shape changed and no flag moved. Reverting would restore the defect, so no `BACKEND_*=rust` may be enabled without this change.

### E-063 · B-026 + G-021: gift details on personal links, the host's static QRIS image, exact copy (s29; D-047)
- **Behaviour before (`2c5fcde`):** every account (number, holder, note) rendered on all five layouts for every audience, the forwardable invitation link included; no copy control; no QRIS image.
- **Change (D-047):**
  - Config (additive, no migration): `gift.personalOnly` (absent = off) and `gift.qrisImageUrl` (a protected upload only; `''` = removed) in `lib/invitation/detail-config.ts`; the content contract lists the QRIS as rendered.
  - Audience before serialization: `lib/invitation/gift-privacy.ts` (`configForAudience`, `ownQrisUpload`, `withNewInvitationGiftDefaults`), applied in `loadPublicInvitation`, `loadPublishedInvitationSummary` (calendar file, link-preview image, child-photo route) and the new `loadInvitationForGiftAudience`. The pages pass `giftWithheld` so the gift block says where the details are (`GiftWithheldNotice`).
  - QRIS delivery: `lib/invitation/gift-qris-delivery.ts` (database-free contract) behind `app/i/[slug]/[guestSlug]/gift/qris/route.ts` and `app/i/[slug]/gift-qris.img/route.ts`; paths in `lib/invitation/public-path.ts`. Upload: `purpose=gift-qris` in `app/api/invitations/[id]/upload/route.ts` (PNG / JPG / WEBP, 1 MB, protected namespace) through `saveImage`'s new allow-list argument (`lib/storage/index.ts`); save-time ownership in `lib/invitation/media-ownership.ts`.
  - Rendering: `GiftQris` (on the palette's lightest surface), `GiftWithheldNotice` and `giftNumberId` in `components/invitation/guest-blocks.tsx`; the copy island `components/invitation/gift-copy.tsx`; the four families and the legacy layout (`public-renderer.tsx`, account markup unchanged); the view model in `components/invitation/guest-view.ts`.
  - Editor (gift tab): the personal-links switch and the QRIS upload. New invitations: `createInvitation` writes `personalOnly: true`.
  - Acceptance: `tests/b026-gift-privacy.test.ts` (new, 14 tests) and `scripts/verify-gift-privacy.ts` (new), which runs as the step "gift privacy on the served build (B-026)" in `scripts/ci/product-gates.sh` (the job now pins `UPLOAD_ROOT` for the server and the checks).
- **Pre-existing guards that caught the first draft (fixed in the code, not in the guards, except one inventory):**
  - `content-contract.test.ts` (a rendered field must be read by the guest page): the QRIS was read only inside `gift-privacy.ts`; `ownQrisUpload` now takes the stored path, so the guest view reads `config.gift?.qrisImageUrl` itself.
  - B-014's and B-008's source checks: the loader's `includeDraft` parameter and the summary loader's own draft check are kept.
  - B-016 (fixed colours only as status pairs in the shared blocks): the QRIS plate's `bg-white` became the palette's lightest surface (`field` on light palettes, `ink` on dark ones), chosen from `palette()` so B-010's single palette source holds; a new test asserts that surface is light (relative luminance ≥ 0.75) on all 18 palettes.
  - e047's upload inventory (three protected of seven) was updated, as B-026 adds one protected upload by design: the three child photos plus the gift tab's QRIS field of eight, still exact, and the route's QRIS branch is asserted to use the protected namespace.
- **Found by the served check:** the QRIS route's `referrer-policy: no-referrer` never reached the response, because the site-wide header in `next.config.ts` replaces route values. It was removed from the route (on an image it governs nothing) and the check asserts the served `strict-origin-when-cross-origin`. D-040's child-photo route has the same harmless override; recorded, not changed.
- **Evidence (s29):**
  - Unit: `tests/b026-gift-privacy.test.ts` **14 / 14**: privacy on every layout, query-free audiences, personal and token links, the setting off, the host preview, **D-041 legacy byte identity** (hashes captured from the pre-change renderer: personal `0c70a979…`, link and preview `36364402…`), QRIS ownership, schema, save-time ownership, the new-only default, the delivery contract, exact copy and the Clipboard fallback, the upload allow-list, the plate on every palette.
  - App job (`scripts/ci/local.sh app`, disposable copy): typecheck, **401 / 401** unit tests, production build with the proxy.
  - Served (`scripts/verify-gift-privacy.ts` against the measurement build of a fresh copy and database, in the product-gates job): **57 / 57**. The invitation link of a personal-only invitation carries no number, digit run, holder, note, QRIS reference or storage path in its HTML, head metadata, RSC payload, calendar file or link-preview image, also with `?guest=`, `?to=`, `?guestSlug=`, `?token=`, `?preview=`, `?ownerPreview=` and `?rsvp=`; personal and opaque-token links show everything through their own QRIS route with the exact bytes and `private, no-store`; a tokened guest's slug, unknown guests, drafts and another invitation's upload answer 404; the legacy layout behaves the same (no copy row); a stored invitation without the setting keeps today's visibility; switching the section off, removing the QRIS, turning the setting on, deleting the guest and rotating the link take effect on the next request; `/media/protected/*` without a session is a private 404 and the preview without a session shows nothing. No rows or objects left.
  - Product gates on the B-026 tree (fresh copy and database): 404s, gift privacy 57 / 57, product bytes **PASS** for wedding, programme and music (budgets unchanged; the copy island is on the families' wedding pages), design acceptance **174 / 174**, cover fold **90 / 90**, rendered contrast **9 / 9**, cover gate **69 / 69**.
- **Limits:** a signed-in host's preview and the upload route itself are not exercised over HTTP (real Clerk keys, DA-01, NOT RUN); the preview's render and the upload allow-list are unit-tested. Real phones, in-app browsers' Clipboard behaviour and a scan of a real bank QRIS are not run (EXT).
- **Risk / rollback:** additive config keys; setting `personalOnly` off restores today's behaviour per invitation; a QRIS image is deleted from the editor. Reverting the code leaves stored keys unread (the schema accepts them as optional).

### E-064 · F-02: new invitations start on the family designed for their event type (s29; D-048)
- **Behaviour before (`2c5fcde`):** `createInvitation` stored the template's preset as it was. No catalog template names a family, so every new invitation rendered the legacy layout, whose cover headline over a photo is the contrast gate's INCOMPLETE case (D-041).

  - `lib/invitation/create.ts`: the only call, after the preset is validated. The editor's "Klasik" option still clears the family.
  - Harness: seeder flags `--new-invitations` (one page per event type, built from its first catalog template) and `--new-invitation-palettes` (every family × palette a catalog template can start a new invitation on: Gapura 11, Riang 15, Lontar 9); the product-gates step "rendered contrast: new invitations (F-02)"; the in-repo harness skill names both and `defaultFamilyFor`.
- **Evidence (s29):**
  - Unit: `tests/f02-default-family.test.ts` **7 / 7**: the six types map to the family designed for them and nothing else maps; the preset is kept and not mutated; flag off starts on legacy; every catalog template (≥ 60) starts a strict-schema-valid invitation on its type's family with its own palette; each type's page renders its family, not legacy; only creation calls it; legacy stays selectable.
  - Jobs on the F-02 tree: app (typecheck, **408 / 408**, build with the proxy), database and rust **PASS**; product gates **PASS** (09:03–09:13Z): 404s, gift privacy 57 / 57, bytes × 3, design 174 / 174, fold 90 / 90, contrast 9 / 9, **new invitations 6 / 6** (0 FAIL, 0 INCOMPLETE), cover gate 69 / 69.
  - One-off sweep (`tools/template-harness/.out/s29/f02-sweep.sh`, ignored; log `/vercel/share/work/logs/s29-f02-sweep.log`) on the same measurement build: **35 / 35 PASS**, 0 FAIL, 0 INCOMPLETE (Lighthouse 12.8.2, Chrome for Testing 158), i.e. no start a new invitation can take is unmeasurable or failing.
- **Limits:** creating an invitation over HTTP needs a signed-in host (DA-01, NOT RUN); the creation path is covered from the stored preset to the rendered page. The sweep uses the review content; a host's own photo is bounded by `worstContrastOverImage`, not by axe.
- **Risk / rollback:** `FLAG_DEFAULT_FAMILY=off` restores the legacy start; invitations created meanwhile keep their family, which the host can switch to "Klasik".

### E-066 · B-018: one builder for guest WhatsApp links, with the host's message (s29; D-050)
- **Behaviour before (`2c5fcde`), confirmed in code:** the per-invitation guest list linked `wa.me/<raw digits>` (QA-013: `08…` never became `62…`) with no message and no personal link; the general list used `toWhatsAppNumber` (any digit string: `+1 415…` → `621415…`, `0062 812…` → `62062812…`, `123` → a link) and a fixed message; the host's `whatsappTemplate` was read nowhere.
- **Change (D-050):** `lib/invitation/whatsapp.ts` (new: `parseWhatsAppNumber`, `toWhatsAppNumber`, `whatsAppMessage`, `guestWhatsApp`); the old normaliser removed from `lib/invitation/public-url.ts`; `components/dashboard/guest-whatsapp-action.tsx` (new) on both lists (`/dashboard/guests`, `/dashboard/invitations/<id>/guests`), which shows "Nomor WhatsApp tidak valid" instead of a link that would fail; the general list's path line now shows the personal link segment (a tokened guest's slug answers 404 under B-011); the editor's WhatsApp tab explains `{nama}` / `{link}`; `docs/systems/wa-delivery.md` documents the manual send.

### E-071 · B-035: version-checked saves, autosave and the side-by-side owner preview, with the editor's content write behind `BACKEND_CONTENT` and its Rust writer (s32; D-053)
- **Behaviour before (`25ed366`), traced (s31's trace, confirmed on the recovered code):** the editor kept the whole config in client state; "Simpan" sent it to `PATCH /api/invitations/[id]/detail` → `updateDetailConfig` (one transaction: config, `max(version_no)`, every version row set off, version + 1; D-040 and B-041 refusals with paths only). No expected-version check, so two tabs overwrote each other; the preview opened in a new tab; one TypeScript writer.
- **Change (D-053):**
  - Rules: `lib/invitation/content-rules.ts` (a stale base first, then D-040's references sorted by path and reason, then B-041's theme; `contentNeedsTier`), the Rust port `services/api/src/content/rules.rs`, shared vectors `contracts/vectors/content-rules.json` (39 decisions: 19 writes, 3 conflicts, 12 media, 5 theme; 20 media-path samples; expected outputs computed by the live TypeScript rules).
  - Writers: `lib/invitation/content-next.ts` and `services/api/src/content/http.rs` (`POST /v1/invitations/{invitation}/content`) lock the owner's row, read `max(version_no)` under the lock, decide, then write the config, the live flag (only the live row turns off) and the next version row in one transaction; `lib/invitation/content-api.ts` (one retry under the same `Idempotency-Key`, then "unconfirmed", codes-only logs). The facade `lib/invitation/content.ts` (`BACKEND_CONTENT`, default `next`): one statement reads config and version, an early conflict, the content contract (`prepareConfigWrite`), exactly one writer and no fallback. `updateDetailConfig` remains as a wrapper over the facade for the verification scripts.
  - Route: `app/api/invitations/[id]/detail/route.ts`: GET answers `version`; PATCH takes `expectedVersion` (absent for a page loaded before B-035: checked against the version read) and answers 409 `VERSION_CONFLICT` with `currentVersion`, 401 for a missing session token under `rust`, 503 `SAVE_UNCONFIRMED`.
  - Editor: `lib/invitation/autosave.ts` (the save loop without React: one save in flight, an answer never replaces newer edits, 800 ms debounce, retries at 2 / 4 / 8 / 15 s, a conflict stops saving until the host chooses, a refusal is not retried with the same content); `app/(customer)/dashboard/invitations/[id]/edit/editor-save.tsx` (the request, a status line with a single `aria-live` copy, the conflict banner with "Muat versi terbaru" / "Simpan versi di tab ini", the preview pane); `editor.tsx` and `page.tsx` wired; flag `EDITOR_AUTOSAVE` (off).
  - Preview: `app/preview/[id]/page.tsx` with `?embed=1` leaves out the owner banner and renders the same `PublicInvitationRenderer`; `proxy.ts` sends `frame-ancestors 'self'` for `/preview/` only (every other page keeps `'none'`; `X-Frame-Options: SAMEORIGIN` unchanged).
  - Migration 0020 (additive): index `invitation_versions (invitation_id, version_no)`, not unique.
  - OpenAPI, `lib/api/schema.d.ts` and `.sqlx` regenerated (`config` is a free-form object, `additionalProperties: true`; a bare object had typed it `Record<string, never>`); `lib/api/client.ts` and `services/api/src/problem.rs` carry `currentVersion` and `issues`; `scripts/verify-content-api.ts` and its step in `scripts/ci/rust.sh`. Docs: `services/api/README.md`, `docs/DATABASE_SCHEMA.md`, `.env.example`, `../09-masterplan/07-canonical-flows.md`, DA-01 item 5.
- **Writer inventory (ADR-A2):** `invitations.config` and `invitation_versions` are written only by the two content writers and by `lib/invitation/create.ts` (the new row and its version 1 in one transaction); `lib/invitation/publish.ts` and `lib/payment/service.ts` write `status`, `published_at` and `expires_at` only, and payment stays with its owner until U6; verification scripts delete their own fixture rows. A source guard in `tests/b035-content-writer.test.ts` keeps this inventory.

Drizzle leaves columns unqualified in a single-table select, so the subquery compared `invitation_versions.invitation_id` with its own `invitation_versions.id` and loaded a wrong version whenever the ids differed: the first save after reopening an edited invitation met a false conflict. Flag off **4 / 4**: no pane, the preview in a new tab, nothing saved without "Simpan", "Simpan" stores with the version check.

### E-072 · B-036: free theme switching within the paid tier, through both content writers (s32 turn 3; D-054)
- **Behaviour before (the B-035 code of 15:39Z), traced:** `themeChangeIssues` (TypeScript) and `theme_change_issues` (Rust) refused any change of `config.theme.templateId` away from `invitations.template_id` as `theme-switch` (403 `THEME_NOT_ENTITLED`; `content.theme_not_entitled` on the API), pinned by 5 theme vectors; the editor's theme tab showed the id read-only ("Fitur ganti tema menyusul"). ## Session 34 (2026-11-07; checkpoint `33c0b12`, tree `a81fadd`; B-024 + I-01 / I-27 finished and its exposure decision recorded)

### E-074 · B-024 + I-01 / I-27: the guest pass, the host's scanner with an offline outbox and a greeting, one arrival per guest through both roster writers (code s33, completed s34; D-056, ADR-A3)
- **State at `33c0b12` (inspected, then measured):** the export held migration 0022 (`guests.pass_id`), the pass rules and vectors in both languages, `POST /v1/invitations/{invitation}/guest-pass-checkins`, the facade write `checkInWithPasses` with both writers, the same-origin route, the scanner (camera, handheld field, manual fallback, outbox, greeting), the guest's pass page, `FLAG_GUEST_PASS` (off) and two test files. Code cited D-056 and ADR-A3; the decision log had neither. `scripts/verify-guest-pass-api.ts`, cited as the rust job's database flow, did not exist. Measured: typecheck 0; unit **558 / 559** (the failure: the API's definite 503 `guest_pass.not_configured` was resent and reported "unconfirmed").
- **Defects found in s34 and fixed:**
  1. **A definite refusal resent.** The Rust client treated every 5xx as an unknown outcome. `guest_pass.not_configured` comes before any write, so it is now definite (`DEFINITE_5XX` in `lib/invitation/roster-api.ts`): one request, answered "not configured".
  2. **Replay parity.** After a lost answer the TypeScript writer (the default) answered `already` where the Rust writer replays `checked-in`, so the usher saw a false amber screen and the writers disagreed; it also ignored the batch key (no `KEY_REUSED`). New `lib/idempotency.ts` is the TypeScript side of `services/api/src/idempotency.rs`: the same table, scope, request hash, statements and retention. `passCheckinNext` looks the key up, records the answer in the write's own transaction, and answers a concurrent twin from its record. The route admits printable ASCII only, so a batch hashes to the same bytes in both writers (vectors `requests`).
  3. **One arrival recorded twice.** The manual fallback reused the guest list's `checkin`, which in both writers overwrote `checked_in_at` and inserted another CHECKIN row on every call. Both writers now store the first time only (`… and checked_in_at is null`, with an event only when a row changed). The manual fallback answers `already` with the stored time, and the scanner shows it amber.
  4. **The camera denied everywhere.** `next.config.ts` sent `Permissions-Policy: camera=()` on every path, so the scanner's camera could never open on a deployment; s34 measured that header on the scanner path before the fix. The scanner path now gets `camera=(self)` from a later header entry. Both links to it (the guest list, the manage centre's Check-in card) load the page in full, because a client-side navigation keeps the previous document's policy.
  - **Smaller corrections:**
    - The outbox keeps a stored batch only when all its scans survived, since a resend must be the same batch.
    - The greeting screens put white text on emerald-700 / rose-700, which gives ≥ 4.5:1 (the 600s gave less).
    - The shells' closed mobile drawers are `inert`: axe `aria-hidden-focus` showed hidden links in the tab order on every dashboard page.
    - The scanner's small label uses brand-700.
    - The scan-time doc comments now state the pinned rule in both languages (an older scan is stored a day before now, not "now"), and the OpenAPI description was regenerated.
    - The manage centre's flag-on copy replaces "roadmap".
    - `cargo fmt` drift in the two B-024 Rust test files, already present at `33c0b12`, is formatted.
- **Exposure (ADR-A3):** the browser never calls the API. The scanner posts to `/api/invitations/<id>/checkins`, which checks Fetch Metadata or Origin before reading the session, accepts JSON only, needs the owner's session and the flag, and limits batches per host and invitation (`RATE_LIMIT_CHECKIN`, 120 / 60 s) before the facade picks one writer. No CORS anywhere.
- **Evidence (s34):**
  - **Unit:** `tests/b024-checkin-writer.test.ts` and `tests/b024-guest-pass.test.ts` **18 / 18**. They cover the definite 503, key reuse, the stored-batch rule, the idempotency statements equal in both languages, the first-arrival rule in both writers and the manual fallback, printable-ASCII canonical bytes, and the camera policy with full-load entries. Full suite and typecheck: see the jobs.
  - **Rust** on a migrated scratch database: fmt clean after formatting; clippy `-D warnings` clean; **111** service tests; contract current (`openapi.json`, `schema.d.ts`; one `.sqlx` query replaced by the first-arrival update).
  - **`verify-guest-pass-api` 24 / 24** (new; rust job step "guest passes end to end"; both writers against the binary; only Next's request primitives and Clerk's session stubbed). Checks:
    - every kind of scan answered alike, with the same rows (three arrivals, one CHECKIN event each, `source: pass`);
    - a pass scanned again through the other writer is `already` with the first time to the millisecond;
    - an answer lost after the commit is resent once under the batch key and replayed `checked-in`;
    - a resent batch replays exactly, to the same writer and across writers;
    - a used key refuses another batch in both writers;
    - five pairs of desks scanning one guest at once give one `checked-in`, one `already` and one event;
    - the manual fallback, the list and a scan record one arrival;
    - under rust with the API down: "unconfirmed" and nothing written;
    - a missing secret: 503 in both writers, asked once (the binary started again without secrets);
    - opt-in 409, another owner 404, administrator 403, signed out 401;
    - the exposure rules (cross-site, same-site, a foreign Origin and neither header refused before the session is read; simple requests 415; no CORS from the route or the API);
    - the limit 429 across writers;
    - flag off 404 with check-ins kept;
    - no fixture row left.
  - **The same script against the checkpoint's TypeScript** (scratch copy of `33c0b12`, current binary): **5 failed / 19 passed**: the replay, key-reuse, manual-fallback, definite-503 and printable-ASCII checks. It discriminates the defects above.
  - **Browser** (Chrome for Testing 158 headless via puppeteer-core; a scratch copy with `@clerk/nextjs` replaced by a cookie stub, a scratch database, `next dev`, `FLAG_GUEST_PASS=on`, `BACKEND_ROSTER=next`): **19 / 19**, plus the manage-centre entry **1 / 1**.
    - the public page offers no pass; the personal page links to it;

### E-075 · G-044: an invitation's team (co-hosts and ushers) by one-time links; a stored answer only after current access, in both writers (code in the s34 export, completed s35; D-057)
- **State at `1c0b1d7` (inspected, then measured):** the export held migration 0023 (`invitation_members`), `lib/invitation/members.ts` / `members-db.ts` / `join-token.ts`, `services/api/src/members.rs`, the owner's team page and actions, `/gabung/[token]` and `/gabung/terima` with the scoped join cookie, the shared dashboard with member entry and leave, grant-aware checks in the pages, actions and Rust roster / RSVP / wishes handlers, and `FLAG_TEAM_ACCESS` (off). Code cited D-057; the log had none. No member-role vectors, no G-044 acceptance, no gate receipts. Measured: typecheck 0; unit **559 / 564** (stale pins of the pre-G-044 owner checks).
- **Reproduced before fixing** (scratch database, a scratch script outside the repository): an usher checks a guest in, the owner revokes the grant, and the usher replays the identical batch under the same key through the TypeScript writer. The answer was the stored `checked-in` with the guest's name and time. The same key with a changed batch answered `key-reused`, which told a revoked caller that the key existed. The Rust writer had the same order in all twelve of its host writes.
- **Defects fixed:**
  1. **Replay before access in 13 host writes** (`passCheckinNext`; in Rust nine roster handlers, the RSVP override, wish moderation and reply). Both writers now establish the caller's current access to that invitation and capability inside the transaction and only then read the idempotency record; the Rust lookup and replay helpers take the transaction (`services/api/src/idempotency.rs`). A concurrent twin is answered from its record inside the same checked transaction. Guest-level handlers check invitation access before the replay, so a retry after the guest row is gone still replays for a caller who keeps access.
  2. **The global guest list's actions** reached the writers, which check the grant but not the flag; they now ask `accessFor` first, so the flag off is owner-only there too.
  3. **Five read entries** (the guest list, import, insights, wishes and the analytics export) did not refuse an administrator before the guard; they do now.
  4. **Grants held by an account later made an administrator or soft-deleted** counted; `invitationAccess` and the API's identity now admit only a live customer account.
  5. **Stale unit pins** (four files) updated to the grant-aware checks.

### E-078 · I-22: the wishes book, the owner's PDF of the visible wishes (s36; D-060)
- **State at `b4fba39`:** `pdf-lib` 1.17.1 pinned; `lib/invitation/wishes-book.ts` with printable text, wrapping, WIB dates, the A5 plan and a 5,000 ceiling. No renderer, loader, route, page wiring, flag, tests or record. The cover claimed the wishes were "tampil di undangan", false for a closed or hosts-only guestbook.
- **Change:**
  - `lib/invitation/wishes-book.ts`: the inclusion copy ("berstatus Tampil", pending and hidden named as left out); emoji reported apart from `[…]`; transliteration marks and typographic variants folded; the invisible set widened to controls, format characters, variation selectors and tags, with LS / PS / NEL as line breaks; wrapping measures each word once (additive widths); the cover title shrinks 26 → 21 → 17 pt and is cut to three lines; the running title fitted by width; the opening fit counts the date gap; a page budget (`WISHES_BOOK_MAX_PAGES` = 2,000) with the cover built from what was printed; `bookCover`, `bookFilename`, `coverNotes`.
  - `lib/invitation/wishes-book-db.ts` (the loader), `lib/invitation/wishes-book-pdf.ts` (the renderer), `app/(customer)/dashboard/invitations/[id]/wishes/book/route.ts`, `wishes/book-card.tsx` and the wishes page's owner-and-flag card, `lib/flags.ts` (`WISHES_BOOK: false`), `.env.example`, `scripts/ci/database.sh` (step "verify-wishes-book (I-22)").
- **Measured and fixed before release:** through pdf-lib's `drawText` and its object-stream save, the longest possible 5,000 wishes (1,000-character messages, each with a 1,000-character reply) took 27.8 s and ≈ 920 MB for 6,251 pages (≈ 45 µs per line drawn; 14.7 s in the save), and 5,000 typical wishes 3.7 s and ≈ 480 MB. The renderer now writes one content stream per page in `drawText`'s operators with pdf-lib's own WinAnsi bytes, deflates with zlib and saves a plain cross-reference table: typical 5,000 wishes **0.99 s, 1.6 MB, 1,668 pages, peak RSS 230 MB**; the longest stop at the budget, **2,000 pages in 1.6 s, 1.9 MB, 327 MB** (1,599 of 5,000 wishes printed, the cover saying so). Same 2-vCPU sandbox for every figure.
- **Evidence (s36):**
  - **Unit** `tests/i22-wishes-book.test.ts` **13 / 13**: printable text (Indonesian, Latin-1 / CP1252, base letters, transliteration, emoji, other scripts, controls, bidirectional and zero-width characters, line breaks); every kept character encodes with a width in all four fonts, and the output stays within WinAnsi over U+0000–U+30FF plus astral samples; wrapping and `fitLine`; the cover, the filename and the copy; the layout with the real fonts (margins, header and folio clearance, order, a reply's bar on both pages); empty, capped, budgeted and long-title books; the rendered PDF read back with zlib and the windows-1252 decoder (text in order, fonts, A5, metadata, no font program or library signature); the two bounded large books; guards (the route's order and headers, the loader's columns and snapshot, pdf-lib only in the renderer and the route, no client component).
  - **`verify-wishes-book` 34 / 34** (new; database job; fresh database from empty): visible wishes only, oldest first with a tie broken by id, the reply, the cover; the headers; no pending, hidden or deleted text, phone, guest link, email, Clerk id, account number, config value, other invitation's wish or join hash in the text, the raw bytes or the headers; closed and hosts-only guestbooks; an empty book; exactly 5,000 (one timestamp, id order) and 5,001 (the oldest 5,000; "5.000 dari 5.001"; ≈ 0.4 s for 835 pages); signed out 401, an administrator holding a grant 403, a co-host, an usher, a revoked member, an outsider and another invitation 404, five malformed ids 404; the other owner's own book; the card for the owner only; a link after the invitation changed hands 404; nothing written; the flag off and on.
  - **Independent parser** (poppler 24.08, scratch): `pdfinfo` reads PDF 1.7, A5 and four non-embedded Type 1 WinAnsi fonts; `pdftotext` reads the content; `pdftotext -bbox`: 0 of 1,080, 227,100 and 448,754 words outside the margins (hostile sample, typical cap, longest); pages inspected as 70 dpi images.
  - **Browser 11 / 11** (the build of E-077): the card counting six visible wishes; the link's accessible name ending in "(PDF)"; the download `buku-ucapan-s36-nikah.pdf` (4 pages) read by poppler with the cover, the six wishes in order, the reply and the folded ʿ; no pending, hidden or phone text; the empty book (one page); a co-host's page without the card and 404 at the route; the owner's rendered link answering 404 after the browser's account changed and 401 after sign-out; flag off (no card, 404).
  - **Build:** the route compiled; 0 of 36 client chunks contain pdf-lib.
- **Limits:** the signed-in path with real Clerk keys is DA-01 item 10, NOT RUN. Latin scripts only; `[…]` for others, no emoji.
- **Risk / rollback:** `FLAG_WISHES_BOOK=off` (the default) removes the card and answers 404; no schema change; nothing is stored.

The crawl harness (scratch) was rebuilt for the combined tree: B-022's pages in both locales, B-031's deleted links and media (a new `deleted` group), the owner list's delete and restore actions, the editor's side-by-side preview (`FLAG_EDITOR_AUTOSAVE`), and controls rewritten where s37's were weak.

  - **Harness corrections found by the crawl (no product change):** an inline script inserted from DevTools-evaluated code is not page script, so the injection controls serve the server's own response with an inline script and an inline handler added (its CSP headers kept); Chrome reports `frame-ancestors` with the framed document's origin only (route `/`); the editor's preview pane needs `FLAG_EDITOR_AUTOSAVE` and an open section; two crawl loops once overlapped (orphans of a stopped shell), so the runner takes an exclusive lock before its exit trap.
  - **Run A:** every mode pages **805 / 0**, controls **23 / 1**: the collector control stopped waiting at four lines (Chrome sends the handler's report twice in report-only) and, in guest and true, the `/pricing` handler report had not arrived.
  - **Run B** (each page's two reports awaited up to 15 s before its context closes; the final wait names all four pairs): unset **805 / 0 + 24 / 0**, guest **805 / 0 + 24 / 0**, true **805 / 0 + 23 / 1** (the `/pricing` handler report missing again).
  - **CDP experiment (enforce, the same injected responses):** both pages fire `securitypolicyviolation` for the blocked script and handler; Chrome's Reporting API delivers the `script-src-elem` report within a second and keeps the handler's report (queued after the image's 404) `Queued` for its next delivery window, still queued at 20 s; on closing the context all four reached the collector, which logged the `/pricing` handler report correctly. Browser delivery batching, not the policy or the collector.
  - **Run C** (true; each page held open until both of its reports arrive, up to 75 s; same assertions): **805 / 0 + 24 / 0**, all four reports delivered while the pages were open.
  - **Final receipt per mode:** unset (run B), guest (run B) and true (run C) **805 / 0 page checks and 24 / 0 controls**, 126 routes: headers 126 (the disposition's policy, a well-formed nonce never reused, `frame-ancestors`, both report directives, `Reporting-Endpoints`, the static headers, `upgrade-insecure-requests` only when enforced over HTTPS), marketing 180, auth 10, guest 216, gallery 78, deleted 25, wizard 11, owner 91, scanner 6, admin 60, collector 2 (status, hydration, the page's probe, no violation in any frame, no uncaught exception, no unexplained console error or request blocked by CSP, no browser report during the page crawl); controls 11 (injected script and handler blocked when enforced and run but reported when report-only, all four reaching the collector as minimized lines with the right disposition; same-origin framing of `/features` refused when enforced, with its report; a cross-origin frame of a guest page refused in every mode; the gallery probe failing when the media origin is blocked; the camera refused outside the scanner; the owner list's delete and restore actions without a violation) and collector 13 (content types, size limits, caps, minimized lines, the `no-store` answer).

- **Limits:** the report-only window on real traffic, real Clerk (DA-01), guest-first then full enforcement in production are owner operations, NOT RUN. Chrome's Reporting API batches reports: a later report waits for the next delivery window and one still queued when its page or frame closes may be lost; a cross-origin frame's report after the frame closes is not delivered (s37). `style-src 'unsafe-inline'` and any-https images and media by design (D-061, D-045).

### E-082 · B-039 (provider-neutral): settlement through one writer per call, the entitlement outbox and checkout idempotency (s39; D-064)
- **State before (U5 freeze 2, `f247112`):** one settlement writer (`applySettlement`, TypeScript) moving the locked order without a conditional transition, no outbox, no Rust payment code, and a checkout that wrote a new PENDING order with a new reference on every submit (s38 inventory, `docs/systems/payment.md`).
- **Change (s39, on `d3d87e5`):**
  - **Shared decision:** `lib/payment/settlement-decision.ts` and `services/api/src/payment/decision.rs`, pinned by `contracts/vectors/payment-settlement.json` (31 cases) through `tests/payment-settlement.test.ts` and `services/api/tests/payment_vectors.rs`.
  - **Next.js writer and facade:** `applySettlement` on the decision, conditional transitions whose row counts are asserted, typed refusals (`lib/payment/errors.ts`), the `payment_settled` row inserted in the transaction after the activation; `settle` selects the writer (`BACKEND_PAYMENT` in `lib/backend.ts`, default `next`); `lib/payment/settlement-api.ts` calls the API with one key per settlement and one resend after an unknown outcome. The webhook route and `confirmManually` call `settle`; the route answers 409 for a rail mismatch and 500 for an unconfirmed settlement; the console says whether the invitation went live and handles both new errors.
  - **Rust writer:** `services/api/src/payment/{mod,decision,http,outbox}.rs`, the signed route `POST /v1/payments/settlements`, `payment_settled` in `jobs.rs`, `openapi.json` and `lib/api/schema.d.ts` regenerated, nine new `.sqlx` descriptors, `services/api/tests/payment_database.rs`.
  - **Checkout key:** migration `0027_checkout_key` (two nullable `varchar(64)` columns, a partial unique index); `lib/payment/checkout-key.ts`; `createOrder` keyed (`onConflictDoNothing`, then resume, paid, closed or conflict); the checkout page renders a key (a pending order keeps its own) and shows the changed, closed and paid notices; the action maps the two new errors and sends Stripe customers to the hosted page.
  - **Tests and CI:** `tests/b039-payment-writer.test.ts`; the B-031 pin in `tests/b031-retention.test.ts` now also requires the conditional activation and the outbox row after it; `scripts/ci/database.sh` runs `verify-checkout-key`; `scripts/ci/rust.sh` step "payment end to end" runs `verify-payment-api` with `REQUIRE_JOBS_BIN=1` and fails on any `SKIP`.
  - **Docs:** `docs/systems/payment.md` (inventory after B-039, settlement, outbox, checkout key, rollout and rollback), `services/api/README.md` (endpoint, problems, keys, outbox, roll-forward runbook), `docs/DATABASE_SCHEMA.md` (0027), `.env.example` (`BACKEND_PAYMENT`).
- **Evidence (s39, the work tree on `d3d87e5`; the final-tree gate receipt is in the session-39 worksheet):**
  - typecheck 0; unit **684 / 684** (641 before, 43 added); services/api **135 / 135** (125 before: 6 unit, 2 payment database and 2 payment vector tests added).
  - `verify-payment` **10 / 10**; `verify-checkout-key` **12 / 12** (five simultaneous taps with one key make one order and one QRIS code, the others resume it; a changed intent conflicts and writes nothing; a closed attempt is refused; another customer's key never reaches the order).
  - `verify-payment-api` **56 / 56** through the real binary on a fresh database: every scenario once per writer (23 Next.js and 26 Rust checks: activation for twelve months with one payment and one outbox row, duplicates through either writer, a later event on a paid order kept without effect, an unknown reference, another rail, an underpayment, another currency, PAID without an amount, FAILED closing without a payment or outbox row, deleted, restored and purged invitations, a theme switched in flight, a legacy order, a running window, six different events at once paying once, one delivery four times at once applying once, a deletion racing the settlement for 8 rounds, the settlement waiting on a deletion that holds the lock and then commits (money recorded, nothing activated) or rolls back (activated), the administrator's confirmation twice, the signed Stripe webhook and its replay, a tampered body 400, another amount 409, an unknown reference 400); **parity: 18 scenarios leave identical outcomes and rows**; the Rust key replays a committed answer, refuses another body (422) and covers an answer lost after the commit; an order paid by Rust is read as paid by Next.js and refused again by its writer; outbox: 43 rows for 43 paid orders, delivered in one pass, delivered again after an expired lease, writing nothing to orders, payments or invitations.
  - **Mutation checks (files restored byte for byte):** removing the Rust writer's live-row guard (select and update) fails 5 checks (deleted, restored, purged, the forced-order commit, parity); the free race alone still passed, since it resolved deletion-first in all 8 rounds, which is why the forced orders exist. Removing `createOrder`'s conflict clause aborts `verify-checkout-key` on `orders_user_checkout_key_idx` (the index refuses the second order).
  - **Populated 0026 → 0027 upgrade, 17 / 17 steps** (fresh database `s39_up27_044318_42086`): the U5 tree (`f247112`) migrated through 0026 and filled it with its own seeds and `verify-payment`, plus two U5-shaped PENDING orders; the B-039 tree applied 0027 (28 migrations). Seven tables (users, templates, invitations, orders, payments, payment events, jobs) identical on the U5 column set (count and md5); two nullable `varchar(64)` columns and the partial unique index; no legacy row keyed. On that database `verify-payment` 10 / 10, `verify-checkout-key` 12 / 12 and `verify-payment-api` 56 / 56; each legacy PENDING order settles once through one writer (one payment, one outbox row) and its redelivery through the other writer is a duplicate. **Code rollback:** the U5 tree's `verify-payment` 10 / 10 and `verify-theme-entitlement` 27 / 27; U5 code reads both writers' payments as paid, settles an order B-039 wrote with a key (key and intent kept, no outbox row) and still writes keyless orders beside the index. **Roll-forward:** the three rows an older runner gives up are released by the reset, the two orders the U5 writer paid are enqueued once (2, then 0 on a second run), the B-039 runner delivers every row, `verify-payment` 10 / 10.
- **Limits:** the provider-specific PJP adapter, a real Stripe Checkout Session, real money, real-Clerk acceptance of the checkout form and the console (DA-01 item 12) and the `BACKEND_PAYMENT=rust` rollout with its SLO window are owner operations, NOT RUN. Payments made before B-039 have no outbox row.
- **Risk / rollback:** `BACKEND_PAYMENT=next` (the default); migration 0027 additive; code rollback and roll-forward rehearsed with stored data from both writers (runbook in `services/api/README.md`).

Brief: task-based help for shipped editor and guest operations; workspace, receipts and coupons only where their flags permit; actual UI terms and routes; ID / EN, accessible navigation, links and flag on / off verified; PJP, credit prices, payouts and other pending capabilities never published.
- **Change (s42):**
  - `lib/help/articles.ts`: seven guides (`HelpArticle`: English slug, topic, `needs`, `routes`, `uiTerms`, ID / EN copy) and `publishedHelpArticles(env)` = the guides whose every capability is `available` (`claimStatus`). Coverage:

    | Guide | Topic | Needs | Default |

    | `create-your-first-invitation` | getting started | themeCatalog, themeSwitch, draftPreview, liveEdits | published |
    | `add-guests-and-send-personal-links` | guests | guestList, personalLinks, whatsappLinks, rsvp | published |
    | `follow-rsvps-and-opens` | guests | rsvp, insights, openTracking, followUpLists, guestCsvExport, whatsappLinks | published |
    | `moderate-and-reply-to-wishes` | guests | wishes | published |
    | `download-a-payment-receipt` | payments | receipts (`FLAG_RECEIPTS`) | 404 until on |
    | `use-a-coupon-code` | payments | coupons (`FLAG_COUPONS`) | 404 until on |
    | `run-a-reseller-workspace` | workspace | resellerWorkspace (`FLAG_WORKSPACES`), checkInScanner (`FLAG_GUEST_PASS`), whatsappLinks | 404 until both on |

The generic `app/resources/[slug]` leaves `help-center` to its own route (`OWN_ROUTES`).

- **Evidence (s42, the work tree after freeze 1; product files identical to the scratch copy below):**

The G-061 file checks: both languages complete with matching step and note counts; each `need` is a built capability; publication follows each guide's flags exactly (on → published, any one off → unpublished; the workspace guide also waits for `FLAG_GUEST_PASS`); the default deployment publishes exactly the four default-on guides; every “quoted” label is listed with the file that renders it and that file contains it; every route resolves to a page under `app/`; any flag-gated word (workspace, receipt, coupon, QR / check-in, co-host, import, seating, music, countdown, export …) is backed by a capability the guide names; no PJP, acquirer, commission, payout, credit, top-up, white-label or affiliate wording; the index reads only `publishedHelpArticles()` and the article page `notFound()`s the rest.
